Twistor Geometry of Null Foliations in Complex Euclidean Space
Arman TAGHAVI-CHABERT

Abstract
We give a detailed account of the geometric correspondence between a smooth complex projective quadric hypersurface 𝒬 n \mathcal{Q}^{n} of dimension n ≥ 3 n\geq 3 , and its twistor space ℙ ​ 𝕋 \mathbb{PT} , defined to be the space of all linear subspaces of maximal dimension of 𝒬 n \mathcal{Q}^{n} . Viewing complex Euclidean space ℂ ​ 𝔼 n \mathbb{CE}^{n} as a dense open subset of 𝒬 n \mathcal{Q}^{n} , we show how local foliations tangent to certain integrable holomorphic totally null distributions of maximal rank on ℂ ​ 𝔼 n \mathbb{CE}^{n} can be constructed in terms of complex submanifolds of ℙ ​ 𝕋 \mathbb{PT} . The construction is illustrated by means of two examples, one involving conformal Killing spinors, the other, conformal Killing–Yano 2 2 -forms. We focus on the odd-dimensional case, and we treat the even-dimensional case only tangentially for comparison.



keywords
twistor geometry; complex variables; foliations; spinors

AMS
32L25; 53C28; 53C12

1
Introduction

The
twistor space
\mathbb{PT}
of a smooth complex projective quadric hypersurface
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
of dimension
n=2m+1\geq 3
, is defined to be the space of all
\gamma
-planes
, i.e.,
m
-dimensional linear subspaces of
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
. This is a complex projective variety of dimension
\frac{1}{2}(m+1)(m+2)
equipped with a canonical holomorphic distribution
\mathrm{D}
of rank
m+1
, and maximally non-integrable, i.e.,
\mathrm{T}\mathbb{PT}=[\mathrm{D},\mathrm{D}]+\mathrm{D}
. Here,
\mathrm{T}\mathbb{PT}
denotes the holomorphic tangent bundle of
\mathbb{PT}
. Noting that a smooth quadric can be identified with a complexified
n
-sphere and is naturally equipped with a holomorphic conformal structure, we shall view complex Euclidean space
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}
as a dense open subset of
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
. In this context, we shall prove the following new results holding locally:

•

totally geodetic integrable holomorphic
\gamma
-plane distributions on
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}
arise from
(m+1)
-dimensional complex submanifolds of
\mathbb{PT}
– Theorem
3.5
;

•

totally geodetic integrable holomorphic
\gamma
-plane distributions on
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}
with integrable orthogonal complements arise from
(m+1)
-dimensional complex submanifolds of
\mathbb{PT}
foliated by holomorphic curves tangent to
{\mathrm{D}}
– Theorem
3.6
;

•

totally geodetic integrable holomorphic
\gamma
-plane distributions on
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}
with totally geodetic integrable orthogonal complements arise from
m
-dimensional complex submanifolds of a
1
-dimensional reduction of a subset of
\mathbb{PT}
known as
mini-twistor space
\mathbb{M}\mathbb{T}
– Theorem
3.8
.

Conversely, any such distributions arise in the ways thus described. These findings may be viewed as odd-dimensional counterparts of the work of
[
20
]
, where it is shown that local foliations of a
2m
-dimensional smooth quadric
\mathcal{Q}^{2m}
by
\alpha
-planes
, i.e., totally null self-dual
m
-planes, are in one-to-one correspondence with certain
m
-dimensional complex submanifolds of twistor space, here defined as the space of all
\alpha
-planes in
\mathcal{Q}^{2m}
.

The first two of the above results are
conformally invariant
, and to arrive at them, we shall first describe the geometrical correspondence between
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
and
\mathbb{PT}
in a manifestly conformally invariant manner, by exploiting the vector and spinor representations of the complex conformal group
\mathrm{SO}(n+2,\mathbb{C})
and of its double-covering
\mathrm{Spin}(n+2,\mathbb{C})
. Such a
tractor
or
twistor calculus
, as it is known, builds on Penrose’s twistor calculus in four dimensions
[
29
]
. The more ‘standard’, local and Poincaré-invariant approach to twistor geometry will also be introduced to describe non-conformally invariant mini-twistor space
\mathbb{M}\mathbb{T}
. In fact, a fairly detailed description of twistor geometry in odd dimensions will make up the bulk of this article, and should, we hope, have a wider range of applications than the one presented here. Once our calculus is all set up, our main results will follow almost immediately. The effectiveness of the tractor calculus will be exemplified by the construction of algebraic subvarieties of
\mathbb{PT}
, which describe the null foliations of
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
arising from certain solutions of conformally invariant differential operators.

Another aim of the present article is to distil the
complex
geometry contained in a number of geometrical results on
real
Euclidean space and Minkowski space in dimensions three and four. In fact, our work is motivated by the findings of
[
27
]
and
[
2
]
. In the former reference, the author recasts the problem of finding pairs of analytic conjugate functions on
\mathbb{E}^{n}
as a problem of finding closed null complex-valued
1
-forms, and arrives at a description of the solutions in terms of real hypersurfaces of
\mathbb{C}^{n-1}
. The case
n=3
is of particular interest, and is the focus of the article
[
2
]
: the kernel of a null complex
1
-form on
\mathbb{E}^{3}
consists of a complex line distribution
\mathrm{T}^{(1,0)}\mathbb{E}^{3}
and the span of a real unit vector
\bm{u}
. This complex
2
-plane distribution is in fact the orthogonal complement
\big(\mathrm{T}^{(1,0)}\mathbb{E}^{3}\big)^{\perp}
of
\mathrm{T}^{(1,0)}\mathbb{E}^{3}
, and we can think of
\mathrm{T}^{(1,0)}\mathbb{E}^{3}
as a CR-structure compatible with the conformal structure on
\mathbb{E}^{3}
viewed as an open dense subset of
S^{3}
. The condition that
\big(\mathrm{T}^{(1,0)}\mathbb{E}^{3}\big)^{\perp}
be integrable is equivalent to
\bm{u}
being tangent to a
conformal foliation
, otherwise known as a
shearfree congruence
of curves. To find such congruences, the authors construct the
S^{2}
-bundle of unit vectors over
S^{3}
, which turns out to be a CR hypersurface in
\mathbb{CP}^{3}
. A section of this
S^{2}
-bundle defines a congruence of curves, and this congruence is shearfree if and only if the section is a
3
-dimensional CR submanifold.

There are three antecedents for this result:

1)

there is a one-to-one correspondence between local self-dual Hermitian structures on
\mathbb{E}^{4}{\subset}S^{4}
and holomorphic sections of the
S^{2}
-bundle
\mathbb{CP}^{3}\rightarrow S^{4}
known as the
twistor bundle
– this is a well-known result, see, e.g.,
[
2
,
4
,
14
,
20
,
32
]
;

2)

there is a one-to-one correspondence between local analytic
shearfree congruences of null geodesics
in Minkowski space
\mathbb{M}
and certain complex hypersurfaces of its twistor space, an auxilliary space isomorphic to
\mathbb{CP}^{3}
– this is known as the
Kerr theorem

[
11
,
29
,
31
]
;

3)

there is a one-to-one correspondence between local
shearfree congruences of geodesics
in
\mathbb{E}^{3}
and certain holomorphic curves in its mini-twistor space, the holomorphic tangent bundle of
\mathbb{CP}^{1}\cong S^{2}
– such congruences can also be equivalently described by
harmonic morphisms

[
3
,
36
,
37
]
.

Statements (1) and (2) are essentially the same result once they are cast in the complexification of
\mathbb{E}^{4}
and
\mathbb{M}
.

The analogy between statement (1) and the result of
[
2
]
can be understood in the following terms: in the former case, the integrable complex null
2
-plane distribution
\mathrm{T}^{(1,0)}\mathbb{E}^{4}
defining the Hermitian structure is
totally geodetic
, i.e.,
\nabla_{\bm{X}}\bm{Y}\in\Gamma\big(\mathrm{T}^{(1,0)}\mathbb{E}^{4}\big)
for all
\bm{X},\bm{Y}\in\Gamma\big(\mathrm{T}^{(1,0)}\mathbb{E}^{4}\big)
. In the latter case, the condition that
\bm{u}
be tangent to a shearfree congruence is also equivalent to the complex null line distribution
\mathrm{T}^{(1,0)}\mathbb{E}^{3}
being (totally) geodetic. One could also think of the integrability of both
\mathrm{T}^{(1,0)}\mathbb{E}^{3}
(trivially) and
\big(\mathrm{T}^{(1,0)}\mathbb{E}^{3}\big)^{\perp}
as an analogue of the integrability of
\mathrm{T}^{(1,0)}\mathbb{E}^{4}
.

Finally, statement (3), unlike (1) and (2), breaks conformal invariance, and the additional data fixing a metric on
\mathbb{E}^{3}
induces a reduction of the
S^{2}
-bundle constructed in
[
2
]
to mini-twistor space
\mathrm{T}S^{2}
of (3). Correspondingly, for
\bm{u}
to be tangent to a shearfree congruence of null geodesics, both
\mathrm{T}^{(1,0)}\mathbb{E}^{3}
and
\big(\mathrm{T}^{(1,0)}\mathbb{E}^{3}\big)^{\perp}
must be totally geodetic, which is not a conformally invariant condition.

The structure of the paper is as follows. Section
2
deals with the twistor geometry of a smooth quadric
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
focussing mostly on the case
n=2m+1
. In particular, we give an algebraic description of the canonical distribution on its twistor space. The geometric correspondence between
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
and
\mathbb{PT}
is made explicit. Propositions
2.12
and
2.13
, and Corollary
2.14
give a twistorial articulation of incidence relations between
\gamma
-planes in
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
. The mini-twistor space
\mathbb{M}\mathbb{T}
of complex Euclidean space
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}
is introduced in Section
2.4
. Points in
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}
correspond to embedded complex submanifolds of
\mathbb{PT}
and
\mathbb{M}\mathbb{T}
, and their normal bundles are described in Section
2.5
. The main results, Theorems
3.5
,
3.6
and
3.8
, as outlined above, are given in Section
3
. In each case, a purely geometrical explanation precedes a computational proof. In Section
4
, we give two examples on how to relate null foliations in
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
to complex varieties in
\mathbb{PT}
, based on certain solutions to the twistor equation, in Propositions
4.2
and
4.3
, and the conformal Killing–Yano equation, in Proposition
4.7
. We wrap up the article with Appendix
A
, which contains a description of standard open covers of twistor space and correspondence space.

2
Twistor geometry

We describe each of the three main protagonists involved in this article in turn: a smooth quadric hypersurface in projective space, its twistor space and a correspondence space fibered over them. The projective variety approach is very much along the line of
[
19
,
31
]
, while the reader should consult
[
5
,
9
]
for the corresponding homogeneous space description.

Throughout
\mathbb{V}
will denote an
(n+2)
-dimensional complex vector space. We shall make use of the following abstract index notation: elements of
\mathbb{V}
and its dual
\mathbb{V}^{*}
will carry upstairs and downstairs calligraphic upper case Roman indices respectively, i.e.,
V^{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathbb{V}
and
\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathbb{V}^{*}
. Symmetrisation and skew-symmetrisation will be denoted by round and square brackets respectively, i.e.,
\alpha_{(\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B})}=\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}}+\alpha_{\mathcal{B}\mathcal{A}})
and
\alpha_{[\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}]}=\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}}-\alpha_{\mathcal{B}\mathcal{A}})
. These conventions will apply to other types of indices used throughout this article. We shall also use Einstein’s summation convention, e.g.,
V^{\mathcal{A}}\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}
will denote the natural pairing of elements of
\mathbb{V}
and
\mathbb{V}^{*}
. We equip
\mathbb{V}
with a non-degenerate symmetric bilinear form
h_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}}
, by means of which
\mathbb{V}\cong\mathbb{V}^{*}
: indices will be lowered and raised by
h_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}}
and its inverse
h^{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}}
respectively. We also choose a complex orientation on
\mathbb{V}
, i.e., a complex volume element
\varepsilon_{\mathcal{A}_{1}\ldots\mathcal{A}_{n+2}}
in
\wedge^{n+2}\mathbb{V}
. We shall denote by
G
the complex spin group
\mathrm{Spin}(n+2,\mathbb{C})
, the two-fold cover of the complex Lie group
\mathrm{SO}(n+2,\mathbb{C})
preserving
h_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}}
and
\varepsilon_{\mathcal{A}_{1}\ldots\mathcal{A}_{n+2}}
.

Turning now to the spinor representations of
G
, we distinguish the odd- and even-dimensional cases:

•

n=2m+1
: denote by
\mathbb{S}
the
2^{m+1}
-dimensional irreducible spinor representation of
G
. Elements of
\mathbb{S}
will carry upstairs bold lower case Greek indices, e.g.,
S^{\bm{\upalpha}}\in\mathbb{S}
, and dual elements, downstairs indices. The Clifford algebra
{\mathcal{C}\ell}(\mathbb{V},h_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}})
is linearly isomorphic to the exterior algebra
\wedge^{\bullet}\mathbb{V}
, and, identifying
\wedge^{k}\mathbb{V}
with
\wedge^{2m+3-k}\mathbb{V}
by Hodge duality for
k=0,\ldots,m+1
, it is also isomorphic, as a matrix algebra, to the space
\mathrm{End}(\mathbb{S})
of endomorphisms of
\mathbb{S}
. It is generated by matrices, denoted
\Gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{\upgamma}}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}}{\bm{\upalpha}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}
, which satisfy the
Clifford identity

\displaystyle\Gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.02783pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.02783pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.58055pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98611pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{\upgamma}}}}_{{{(\mathcal{A}}{\bm{\upalpha}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\Gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.71532pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.71532pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.37639pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.84029pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{\upbeta}}}}_{{{\mathcal{B})}{\bm{\upgamma}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}=-h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.49312pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.49312pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.14584pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.10417pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}}}}\delta^{{{\bm{\upbeta}}}}_{{{\bm{\upalpha}}}}.

(2.1)

Here
\delta^{{{\bm{\upbeta}}}}_{{{\bm{\upalpha}}}}
is the identity element on
\mathbb{S}
. There is a spin-invariant inner product on
\mathbb{S}
denoted
\Gamma^{(0)}_{\bm{\updelta\upbeta}}\colon\mathbb{S}\times\mathbb{S}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
, yielding the isomorphism
\mathrm{End}(\mathbb{S})\cong\mathbb{S}\otimes\mathbb{S}
. The resulting isomorphisms
{\mathcal{C}\ell}(\mathbb{V},h_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}})\cong\wedge^{\bullet}\mathbb{V}\cong\mathbb{S}\otimes\mathbb{S}
will be realised by means of the bilinear forms on
\mathbb{S}
with values in
\wedge^{k}\mathbb{V}^{*}
, for
k=1,\ldots,n+2
:

\displaystyle\Gamma^{{{(k)}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}_{1}\ldots\mathcal{A}_{k}}{\bm{\upalpha\upbeta}}}}:=\Gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.11671pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.11671pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.28612pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.84721pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{\upgamma_{1}}}}}_{{{[\mathcal{A}_{1}}{\bm{\upalpha}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.51799pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.51799pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.04935pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.32454pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\cdots\Gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.37192pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.37192pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.4684pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.0295pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.44516pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.44516pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.56876pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.84395pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{\updelta_{k}}}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}_{k}]}{\bm{\upgamma_{k-1}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.1836pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.1836pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.85551pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.23329pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\Gamma^{(0)}_{\bm{\updelta_{k}\upbeta}}.

(2.2)

These are symmetric in their spinor indices when
k\equiv m+1,m+2\pmod{4}
and skew-symmetric otherwise.

•

n=2m
:
G
has two
2^{m}
-dimensional irreducible chiral spinor representations, which we shall denote
\mathbb{S}
and
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
. Elements of
\mathbb{S}
and
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
will carry upstairs unprimed and primed lower case bold Greek indices respectively, i.e.,
A^{\bm{\upalpha}}\in\mathbb{S}
and
B^{\bm{\upalpha^{\prime}}}\in\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
. Dual elements will carry downstairs indices. The Clifford algebra
{\mathcal{C}\ell}(\mathbb{V},h_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}})
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{End}(\mathbb{S}\oplus\mathbb{S}^{\prime})
as a matrix algebra, and, linearly, to
\wedge^{\bullet}\mathbb{V}
. We can write its generators in terms of matrices
\Gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{\upgamma^{\prime}}}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}}{\bm{\upalpha}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.048pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.048pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.99936pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.27455pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}
and
\Gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.04625pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.04625pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.59703pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.70145pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{\upgamma}}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}}{\bm{\upalpha^{\prime}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}
satisfying

\displaystyle\Gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.02783pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.02783pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.58055pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98611pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{\upgamma^{\prime}}}}}_{{{(\mathcal{A}}{\bm{\upalpha}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.048pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.048pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.99936pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.27455pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\Gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.71532pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.71532pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.37639pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.84029pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.048pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.048pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.99936pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.27455pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{\upbeta}}}}_{{{\mathcal{B})}{\bm{\upgamma^{\prime}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}=-h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.49312pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.49312pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.14584pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.10417pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}}}}\delta^{{{\bm{\upbeta}}}}_{{{\bm{\upalpha}}}},\qquad\Gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.02783pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.02783pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.58055pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98611pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.04625pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.04625pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.59703pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.70145pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{\upgamma}}}}_{{{(\mathcal{A}}{\bm{\upalpha^{\prime}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\Gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.71532pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.71532pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.37639pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.84029pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{\upbeta^{\prime}}}}}_{{{\mathcal{B})}{\bm{\upgamma}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3819pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.3819pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.23405pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.44218pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}=-h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.49312pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.49312pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.14584pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.10417pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}}}}\delta^{{{\bm{\upbeta^{\prime}}}}}_{{{\bm{\upalpha^{\prime}}}}},

where
\delta^{{{\bm{\upbeta}}}}_{{{\bm{\upalpha}}}}
and
\delta^{{{\bm{\upbeta^{\prime}}}}}_{{{\bm{\upalpha^{\prime}}}}}
are the identity elements on
\mathbb{S}
and
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
respectively. There are spin-invariant bilinear forms on
\mathbb{S}\oplus\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
inducing isomorphisms
\mathbb{S}^{*}\cong\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
,
(\mathbb{S}^{\prime})^{*}\cong\mathbb{S}
when
m
is even, and
\mathbb{S}^{*}\cong\mathbb{S}
and
(\mathbb{S}^{\prime})^{*}\cong\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
when
m
is odd, and denoted
\Gamma^{{{(0)}}}_{{{\bm{\upalpha\upbeta^{\prime}}}}}
,
\Gamma^{{{(0)}}}_{{{\bm{\upalpha^{\prime}\upbeta}}}}
, and
\Gamma^{{{(0)}}}_{{{\bm{\upalpha\upbeta}}}}
,
\Gamma^{{{(0)}}}_{{{\bm{\upalpha^{\prime}\upbeta^{\prime}}}}}
respectively.
The resulting isomorphisms
{\mathcal{C}\ell}(\mathbb{V},h_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}})\cong\wedge^{\bullet}\mathbb{V}\cong(\mathbb{S}\oplus\mathbb{S}^{\prime})\otimes(\mathbb{S}\oplus\mathbb{S}^{\prime})
are realised by
\wedge^{k}\mathbb{V}
-valued bilinear forms
\Gamma^{{{(k)}}}_{{{\bm{\upalpha}\bm{\upbeta}}}}
, for
k\equiv m+1\pmod{2}
, and
\Gamma^{{{(k)}}}_{{{\bm{\upalpha}\bm{\upbeta^{\prime}}}}}
, for
k\equiv m\pmod{2}
and so on.

We work in the holomorphic category throughout.

2.1
Smooth quadric hypersurface

Let us denote by
X^{\mathcal{A}}
the position vector in
\mathbb{V}
, which can be viewed as standard Cartesian coordinates on
\mathbb{C}^{n+2}
. The equivalence class of non-zero vectors in
\mathbb{V}
that projects down to the same point in the projective space
\mathbb{P}\mathbb{V}\cong\mathbb{CP}^{n+1}
will be denoted
[\cdot]
, and thus
[X^{\mathcal{A}}]
will represent homogeneous coordinates on
\mathbb{P}\mathbb{V}
.

The zero set of the quadratic form associated to
h_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}}
on
\mathbb{V}
defines a null cone
\mathcal{C}
in
\mathbb{V}
, and the projectivisation of
\mathcal{C}
defines a smooth quadric hypersurface
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
in
\mathbb{P}\mathbb{V}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}^{n}=\big\{\big[X^{\mathcal{A}}\big]\in\mathbb{P}\mathbb{V}\colon h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.49312pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.49312pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.14584pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.10417pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}}}}X^{\mathcal{A}}X^{\mathcal{B}}=0\big\}.

By taking a suitable cross-section of
\mathcal{C}
, one can identify
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
with the complexification
\mathbb{C}S^{n}
of the standard
n
-sphere
S^{n}
in Euclidean space
\mathbb{E}^{n+1}
. Using the affine structure on
\mathbb{V}
,
h_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}}
can be viewed as a field of bilinear forms on
\mathbb{V}
and thus on
\mathcal{C}
. We can then pull back
h_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}}
to
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
along any section of
\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{Q}^{n}
to a (holomorphic) metric on
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
. Different sections yield conformally related metrics on
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
, i.e., a (holomorphic) conformal structure on
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
. The
projective tangent space
at a point
p
of
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
with homogeneous coordinate
\big[P^{\mathcal{A}}\big]
is the linear subspace

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}_{p}\mathcal{Q}^{n}:=\big\{\big[X^{\mathcal{A}}\big]\in\mathcal{Q}^{n}\colon h_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}}X^{\mathcal{A}}P^{\mathcal{B}}=0\big\},

which can be seen to be the closure of the (holomorphic) tangent space
\mathrm{T}_{p}\mathcal{Q}^{n}
at
p\in\mathcal{Q}^{n}
in the usual sense. The intersection of
\mathbf{T}_{p}\mathcal{Q}^{n}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
is a cone through
p
, and any point lying in this cone is connected to its vertex by a line that is null with respect to the conformal structure.

To obtain the Kleinian model of
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
, we fix a null vector
\mathring{X}^{\mathcal{A}}
in
\mathbb{V}
, and denote by
P
the stabiliser of the line spanned by
\mathring{X}^{\mathcal{A}}
in
G
. The transitive action of
G
on
\mathbb{V}
descends to a transitive action on
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
, and since
P
stabilises a point in
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
, we obtain the identification
G/P\cong\mathcal{Q}^{n}
. The subgroup
P
is a parabolic subgroup of
G
, and its Lie algebra
\mathfrak{p}
admits a Levi decomposition, that is, a splitting
\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{p}_{0}\oplus\mathfrak{p}_{1}
, where
\mathfrak{p}_{0}
is the reductive Lie algebra
\mathfrak{so}(n,\mathbb{C})\oplus\mathbb{C}
, and
\mathfrak{p}_{1}
is a nilpotent part, here isomorphic to
(\mathbb{C}^{n})^{*}
. We choose a complement
\mathfrak{p}_{-1}
of
\mathfrak{p}
in
\mathfrak{g}
, dual to
\mathfrak{p}_{1}
via the Killing form on
\mathfrak{g}
, so that
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{p}_{-1}\oplus\mathfrak{p}
. There is a unique element spanning the centre
\mathfrak{z}(\mathfrak{p}_{0})\cong\mathbb{C}
of
\mathfrak{p}_{0}
, which acts diagonally on
\mathfrak{p}_{0}
,
\mathfrak{p}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{p}_{-1}
with eigenvalues
0
,
1
and
-1
respectively. For this reason, we refer to this element as the
grading element
of the splitting
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{p}_{-1}\oplus\mathfrak{p}_{0}\oplus\mathfrak{p}_{1}
. This splitting is compatible with the Lie bracket
[\cdot,\cdot]\colon\mathfrak{g}\times\mathfrak{g}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}
on
\mathfrak{g}
in the sense that
[\mathfrak{p}_{i},\mathfrak{p}_{j}]\subset\mathfrak{p}_{i+j}
, with the convention that
\mathfrak{p}_{i}=\{0\}
for
|i|>1
. In particular, it is invariant under
\mathfrak{p}_{0}
, but not under
\mathfrak{p}
. However, the filtration
\mathfrak{p}^{1}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{0}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{-1}:=\mathfrak{g}
, where
\mathfrak{p}^{1}:=\mathfrak{p}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{p}^{0}:=\mathfrak{p}_{0}\oplus\mathfrak{p}_{1}
,
is
a filtration of
\mathfrak{p}
-modules on
\mathfrak{g}
, and each of the
\mathfrak{p}
-modules
\mathfrak{p}^{-1}/\mathfrak{p}^{0}
,
\mathfrak{p}^{0}/\mathfrak{p}^{1}
and
\mathfrak{p}^{1}
is linearly isomorphic to the
\mathfrak{p}_{0}
-modules
\mathfrak{p}_{-1}
,
\mathfrak{p}_{0}
and
\mathfrak{p}_{1}
respectively. These properties are most easily verified by realising
\mathfrak{g}
in matrix form, i.e.,

when
n=2m+1
and
n=2m
respectively.

Given a vector representation
V
of
P
, one can construct the holomorphic homogeneous vector bundle
G\times_{P}V
over
G/P
: this is the orbit space of a point in
G\times V
under the right action of
G
. In particular, the tangent bundle of
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
can be described as
\mathrm{T}(G/P)\cong G\times_{P}\left(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{p}\right)
, and the tangent space at any point of
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
is isomorphic to
\mathfrak{p}_{-1}\cong\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{p}
– for a proof, see, e.g.,
[
9
]
. Similarly, denoting by
P_{0}
the reductive subgroup of
P
with Lie algebra
\mathfrak{p}_{0}
, we can construct holomorphic homogeneous vector bundles from representations of
P_{0}
.

2.1.1
The tractor bundle

An important homogeneous vector bundle over
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
is the one constructed from the standard representation
\mathbb{V}
of
G
. It leads to a conformal invariant calculus, known as
tractor calculus
. The reader should consult, e.g.,
[
1
,
12
]
for further details.

Definition 2.1
.

The
(
complex
)
standard tractor bundle
over
\mathcal{Q}^{n}\cong G/P
is the rank-
(n+2)
vector bundle
\mathcal{T}:=G\times_{P}\mathbb{V}\cong G/P\times\mathbb{V}
.

The symmetric bilinear form
h_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}}
on
\mathbb{V}
induces a non-degenerate holomorphic section of
\odot^{2}\mathcal{T}^{*}\rightarrow\mathcal{Q}^{n}
on
\mathcal{T}
, called the
tractor metric
, also denoted by
h_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}}
. Further, the affine structure on
\mathbb{V}
induces a unique
tractor connection
on
\mathcal{T}
preserving
h_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}}
.

The vector space
\mathbb{V}
admits a filtration of
P
-modules
\mathbb{V}=:\mathbb{V}^{-1}\supset\mathbb{V}^{0}\supset\mathbb{V}^{1}
, where
\mathbb{V}^{1}=\big\langle\mathring{X}^{\mathcal{A}}\big\rangle
and
\mathbb{V}^{0}
is the orthogonal complement of
\mathbb{V}^{1}
. These
P
-modules and their quotients
\mathbb{V}^{i}/\mathbb{V}^{i+1}
give rise to
P
-invariant vector bundles as explained above. For convenience, we choose a splitting

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{V}_{-1}\oplus\mathbb{V}_{0}\oplus\mathbb{V}_{1},

(2.3)

where
\mathbb{V}_{1}:=\mathbb{V}^{-1}
,
\mathbb{V}_{-1}
is a null line in
\mathbb{V}
complementary to
\mathbb{V}^{0}\subset\mathbb{V}^{-1}
, and
\mathbb{V}_{0}
is the
n
-dimensional vector subspace orthogonal to both
\mathbb{V}_{-1}
and
\mathbb{V}_{1}
. We note the linear isomorphisms
\mathbb{V}_{-1}\cong\mathbb{V}^{-1}/\mathbb{V}^{0}
and
\mathbb{V}_{0}\cong\mathbb{V}^{0}/\mathbb{V}^{1}
.

Let us introduce some abstract index notation. Elements of
\mathbb{V}_{0}
and its dual
(\mathbb{V}_{0})^{*}
will be adorned with upstairs and downstairs lower-case Roman indices respectively, e.g.,
V^{a}\in\mathbb{V}_{0}
and
\alpha_{a}\in(\mathbb{V}_{0})^{*}
. We fix a null vector
\mathring{Y}^{\mathcal{A}}
spanning
\mathbb{V}_{-1}
such that
\mathring{X}^{\mathcal{A}}\mathring{Y}_{\mathcal{A}}=1
. We also introduce the injector
\mathring{Z}^{\mathcal{A}}_{a}\colon\mathbb{V}_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{V}
. Then,
h_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}}
restricts to a non-degenerate symmetric bilinear form
g_{ab}:=\mathring{Z}^{\mathcal{A}}_{a}\mathring{Z}^{\mathcal{B}}_{b}h_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}}
on
\mathbb{V}_{0}
. Indices can be raised or lowered by means of
h_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}}
,
g_{ab}
and their inverses.

A geometric interpretation of
\mathcal{T}\rightarrow G/P
can be found in
[
12
]
in a real setting. Here, we note that the line subbundle
G\times_{P}\mathbb{V}^{1}
of
\mathcal{T}
can be identified with the pull-back
\mathcal{O}[-1]
of the tautological line bundle
\mathcal{O}(-1)
on
\mathbb{P}\mathbb{V}
to
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
. The bundle
G\times_{P}\left(\mathbb{V}^{-1}/\mathbb{V}^{0}\right)
is isomorphic to the dual of
\mathcal{O}[-1]
, i.e., to the pullback
\mathcal{O}[1]
of the hyperplane bundle
\mathcal{O}(1)
on
\mathbb{P}\mathbb{V}
. Finally, since
\mathfrak{p}_{-1}\otimes\mathbb{V}_{1}\cong\mathbb{V}_{0}
, we have the identification
G\times_{P}\left(\mathbb{V}^{0}/\mathbb{V}^{1}\right)\cong\mathrm{T}\mathcal{Q}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}[-1]
.

The structure sheaf of
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
will be denoted
\mathcal{O}
, and the sheaf of germs of holomorphic functions on
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
homogeneous of degree
w
by
\mathcal{O}[w]
. We shall write
\mathcal{O}^{a}
for the sheaf of germs of holomorphic sections of
\mathrm{T}\mathcal{Q}^{n}
, and extend this notation in the obvious way to tensor products, e.g.,
\mathcal{O}^{\mathcal{A}}_{ab}[w]:=\mathcal{O}^{\mathcal{A}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{ab}\otimes\mathcal{O}[w]
, and so on. In particular, the sheaf of germs of holomorphic sections of the tractor bundle
\mathcal{T}
reads

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{\mathcal{A}}=\mathcal{O}[1]+\mathcal{O}^{a}[-1]+\mathcal{O}[-1].

(2.4)

The line bundle
\mathcal{O}[1]
has the geometric interpretation of the bundle of
conformal scales
, and the conformal structure on
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
can be equivalently encoded in terms of a distinguished global section
\mathbf{g}_{ab}
of
\mathcal{O}_{(ab)}[2]
called the
conformal metric
. For any non-vanishing local section
\sigma
of
\mathcal{O}[1]
,
g_{ab}=\sigma^{-2}\mathbf{g}_{ab}
is a metric in the conformal class. A choice of metric in the conformal class is essentially equivalently to a splitting of (
2.4
), i.e., a choice of section
Y^{\mathcal{A}}
of
\mathcal{O}^{\mathcal{A}}[-1]
such that
Y^{\mathcal{A}}Y_{\mathcal{A}}=0
and
X^{\mathcal{A}}Y_{\mathcal{A}}=1
, where we view
X^{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathcal{O}^{\mathcal{A}}[1]
as the Euler vector field on
\mathcal{C}\subset\mathbb{V}
. We can then choose a section
Z_{a}^{\mathcal{A}}
of
\mathcal{O}^{\mathcal{A}}_{a}[1]
satisfying
Z_{a}^{\mathcal{A}}Z_{b\mathcal{A}}=\mathbf{g}_{ab}
and
Z_{a}^{\mathcal{A}}X_{\mathcal{A}}=Z_{a}^{\mathcal{A}}Y_{\mathcal{A}}=0
, so that the tractor metric takes the form
h_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}}=2X_{(\mathcal{A}}Y_{\mathcal{B})}+Z_{\mathcal{A}}^{a}Z_{\mathcal{B}}^{b}\mathbf{g}_{ab}
– see, e.g.,
[
15
]
. A section
\Sigma^{\mathcal{A}}
of
\mathcal{O}^{\mathcal{A}}
can be expressed as

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\mathcal{A}}=\sigma Y^{\mathcal{A}}+\varphi^{a}Z_{a}^{\mathcal{A}}+\rho X^{\mathcal{A}},\qquad\text{where}\quad(\sigma,\varphi^{a},\rho)\in\mathcal{O}[1]\oplus\mathcal{O}^{a}[-1]\oplus\mathcal{O}[-1].

(2.5)

We shall denote both the tractor connection and the Levi-Civita connection of a metric in the conformal class by
\nabla_{a}
. The explicit formula for the tractor connection on a section (
2.5
) of
\mathcal{O}^{\mathcal{A}}
in terms of a splitting of (
2.4
) can then be recovered from the Leibniz rule and the formulae

\displaystyle\nabla_{a}X^{\mathcal{A}}=Z^{\mathcal{A}}_{a},\qquad\nabla_{a}Z^{\mathcal{A}}_{b}=-\mathrm{P}_{ab}X^{\mathcal{A}}-\mathbf{g}_{ab}Y^{\mathcal{A}},\qquad\nabla_{a}Y^{\mathcal{A}}=\mathrm{P}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}_{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}Z^{\mathcal{A}}_{b},

(2.6)

where
\mathrm{P}_{ab}
is the Schouten tensor of
\nabla_{a}
defined by the relation
2\nabla_{[a}\nabla_{b]}V^{c}=2\mathrm{P}^{{{c}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.60155pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.60155pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.9512pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82227pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{[a}}}V^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.78056pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.78056pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.46402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.47429pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b]}}}-2V^{d}\mathrm{P}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.76442pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.76442pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.50159pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.64397pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{d[a}}}\delta^{{{c}}}_{{{b]}}}
.

Complex Euclidean space.
Most of this paper will be concerned with the geometry on
n
-dimensional complex Euclidean space
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}
viewed as a dense open subset of
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
, i.e.,
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}=\mathcal{Q}^{n}\setminus\{\infty\}
where
\infty
is a point at ‘infinity’ on
\mathbb{C}S^{n}\cong\mathcal{Q}^{n}
. We choose a conformal scale
\sigma\in\mathcal{O}[1]
so that
g_{ab}
is the flat metric, i.e.,
\mathrm{P}_{ab}=0
. To realise
\sigma
geometrically, we use the splitting (
2.3
). Then,
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}
arises as the intersection of the affine hyperplane
\mathcal{H}:=\{X^{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathbb{V}\colon X^{\mathcal{A}}\mathring{Y}_{\mathcal{A}}=1\}
with
\mathcal{C}
:
\mathbb{V}_{1}=\langle\mathring{X}^{\mathcal{A}}\rangle
descends to the origin on
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}
, and
\mathbb{V}_{-1}=\langle\mathring{Y}^{\mathcal{A}}\rangle
represents
\infty
on
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
. The flat metric
g_{ab}
is obtained by pulling back
h_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}}
along the local section
\mathcal{C}\cap\mathcal{H}
of
\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{Q}^{n}
. Letting
\{x^{a}\}
be flat coordinates on
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}
so that
\nabla_{a}=\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{a}}
, we can integrate (
2.6
) explicitly to get

\displaystyle Y^{\mathcal{A}}=\mathring{Y}^{\mathcal{A}},\qquad Z^{\mathcal{A}}_{a}=\mathring{Z}^{\mathcal{A}}_{a}-g_{ab}x^{b}\mathring{Y}^{\mathcal{A}},\qquad X^{\mathcal{A}}=\mathring{X}^{\mathcal{A}}+x^{a}\mathring{Z}^{\mathcal{A}}_{a}-\tfrac{1}{2}g_{ab}x^{a}x^{b}\mathring{Y}^{\mathcal{A}}.

(2.7)

This description is also consistent with the identification of
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}
with the tangent space at the ‘origin’ of
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
. In this case, the coordinates
\{x^{a}\}
arise from
\mathfrak{p}_{-1}\cong\mathbb{V}_{-1}\otimes\mathbb{V}_{0}
via the exponential map, which provides an embedding of
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}
into
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
,
x^{a}\mapsto[X^{\mathcal{A}}]
where
X^{\mathcal{A}}
is given by (
2.7
). The embedding can in fact be extended to a
conformal
embedding

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{Q}^{n},\\
&x^{a}\mapsto\Omega X^{\mathcal{A}}=\Omega\mathring{X}^{\mathcal{A}}+x^{a}\Omega\mathring{Z}_{a}^{\mathcal{A}}-\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\Omega^{2}g_{ab}x^{a}x^{b}\big)\Omega^{-1}\mathring{Y}^{\mathcal{A}}\mapsto\big[X^{\mathcal{A}}\big],\end{split}

obtained by intersecting
\mathcal{C}
with the affine hypersurface
\mathcal{H}_{\Omega}:=\{X^{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathbb{V}\colon X^{\mathcal{A}}\mathring{Y}_{\mathcal{A}}=\Omega\}
, where
\Omega
is a non-vanishing holomorphic function on
\mathbb{V}
.

2.1.2
The tractor spinor bundle

We can play the same game by considering bundles over
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
arising from the spinor representations of
G=\mathrm{Spin}(n+2,\mathbb{C})
. Again, we distinguish the odd- and even-dimensional cases.

Odd dimensions.
Assume
n=2m+1
.

Definition 2.2
.

The
tractor spinor bundle
and
dual tractor spinor bundle
over
\mathcal{Q}^{n}\cong G/P
are the holomorphic homogeneous vector bundles
\mathcal{S}:=G\times_{P}\mathbb{S}
and
\mathcal{S}^{*}:=G\times_{P}\mathbb{S}^{*}
respectively.

The generators
\Gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{\upbeta}}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}}{\bm{\upalpha}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}
of the Clifford algebra
(\mathbb{V},h_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}})
induce holomorphic sections of
\mathcal{T}^{*}\otimes\mathcal{S}^{*}\otimes\mathcal{S}
on
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
, which we shall also denote by
\Gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{\upbeta}}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}}{\bm{\upalpha}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}
. The tractor connection on
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
extends to a
tractor spinor connection
on
\mathcal{S}
preserving
\Gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{\upbeta}}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}}{\bm{\upalpha}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}
, and thus
h_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}}
.

There is a filtration of
P
-submodules
\mathbb{S}=:\mathbb{S}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\supset\mathbb{S}^{\frac{1}{2}}
. These
P
-modules and their quotients give rise to
P
-invariant vector bundles on
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
in the standard way. The splitting (
2.3
) of
\mathbb{V}
induces a splitting

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}\cong\mathbb{S}_{-\frac{1}{2}}\oplus\mathbb{S}_{\frac{1}{2}},

(2.8)

where
\mathbb{S}_{\frac{1}{2}}\cong\mathbb{V}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{S}_{-\frac{1}{2}}
, and we can identify
\mathbb{S}_{-\frac{1}{2}}
, and thus
\mathbb{S}_{\frac{1}{2}}
, as the spinor representation for
(\mathbb{V}_{0},g_{ab})
. Similar considerations apply to
\mathbb{S}^{*}
. See, e.g.,
[
16
,
17
]
for details.

Elements of
\mathbb{S}_{\pm\frac{1}{2}}
will carry bold upper case Roman indices, e.g.,
\xi^{\mathbf{A}}\in\mathbb{S}_{\pm\frac{1}{2}}
. The Clifford algebra generators
\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{a}{\mathbf{A}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}
satisfy
\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.46266pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.46266pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.49564pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.21117pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{C}}}}_{{{(a}{\mathbf{A}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.06969pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.90692pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.64168pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.64168pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00845pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.06969pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.90692pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{b)}{\mathbf{C}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}=-g_{ab}\delta^{{{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{\mathbf{A}}}}
, where
\delta^{{{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{\mathbf{A}}}}
is the identity on
\mathbb{S}_{\pm\frac{1}{2}}
. There is a spin-invariant bilinear form
\gamma^{{{(0)}}}_{{{\mathbf{AB}}}}
on
\mathbb{S}_{\pm\frac{1}{2}}
, by means of which we can define bilinear forms

\displaystyle\gamma^{{{(k)}}}_{{{a_{1}\ldots a_{k}}{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}}}:=\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.55154pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.55154pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2012pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.07227pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{C_{1}}}}}_{{{[a_{1}}{\mathbf{A}}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.83691pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.83691pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.58217pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.4194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\cdots\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.80675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.80675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.38348pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25455pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.44424pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.44424pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.87311pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.71034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{C_{k}}}}}_{{{a_{k}]}{\mathbf{C_{k-1}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.99344pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.99344pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.69397pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5312pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\gamma^{{{(0)}}}_{{{\mathbf{C_{k}}\mathbf{B}}}},

from
\mathbb{S}_{\pm\frac{1}{2}}\times\mathbb{S}_{\pm\frac{1}{2}}
to
\wedge^{k}\mathbb{V}_{0}
for
k=1,\ldots,n
. We introduce projectors
\mathring{O}_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}\colon\mathbb{S}\rightarrow\mathbb{S}_{-\frac{1}{2}}
and
\mathring{I}_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}\colon\mathbb{S}\rightarrow\mathbb{S}_{\frac{1}{2}}
, and injectors
\mathring{I}^{\bm{\upalpha}}_{\mathbf{A}}\colon\mathbb{S}_{-\frac{1}{2}}\rightarrow\mathbb{S}
and
\mathring{O}^{\bm{\upalpha}}_{\mathbf{A}}\colon\mathbb{S}_{\frac{1}{2}}\rightarrow\mathbb{S}
, which satisfy
\mathring{O}_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{B}}\mathring{I}^{\bm{\upalpha}}_{\mathbf{A}}=\delta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{B}}
and
\mathring{O}_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathring{I}^{\bm{\upbeta}}_{\mathbf{A}}+\mathring{I}_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathring{O}^{\bm{\upbeta}}_{\mathbf{A}}=\delta_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\bm{\upbeta}}
. Then one can check that the relation between
\Gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{\upbeta}}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}}{\bm{\upalpha}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}
and
\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{a}{\mathbf{A}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}
is given by

\displaystyle\Gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{\upbeta}}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}}{\bm{\upalpha}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}=\mathring{Z}_{\mathcal{A}}^{a}\big(\mathring{O}_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathring{I}^{\bm{\upbeta}}_{\mathbf{B}}\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{a}{\mathbf{A}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}-\mathring{I}_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathring{O}^{\bm{\upbeta}}_{\mathbf{B}}\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{a}{\mathbf{A}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\big)+\sqrt{2}\mathring{Y}_{\mathcal{A}}\mathring{O}_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathring{O}^{\bm{\upbeta}}_{\mathbf{A}}-\sqrt{2}\mathring{X}_{\mathcal{A}}\mathring{I}_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathring{I}^{\bm{\upbeta}}_{\mathbf{A}}.

(2.9)

Sheaves of germs of holomorphic sections of
G\times_{P}\big(\mathbb{S}^{-\frac{1}{2}}/\mathbb{S}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big)
will be denoted
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbf{A}}
, and we shall write
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbf{A}}[-1]:=\mathcal{O}^{\mathbf{A}}\otimes\mathcal{O}[-1]
, and similarly for dual bundles in the obvious way. In particular, the sheaves of germs of holomorphic sections of
\mathcal{S}
and its dual are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{\bm{\upalpha}}=\mathcal{O}^{\mathbf{A}}+\mathcal{O}^{\mathbf{A}}[-1],\qquad\mathcal{O}_{\bm{\upalpha}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{A}}[1]+\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{A}},

(2.10)

respectively. The splitting of (
2.10
) can be realised by means of injectors/projectors
O_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}
,
I_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}[-1]
,
O^{\bm{\upalpha}}_{\mathbf{A}}\in\mathcal{O}^{\bm{\upalpha}}_{\mathbf{A}}[1]
and
I^{\bm{\upalpha}}_{\mathbf{A}}\in\mathcal{O}^{\bm{\upalpha}}_{\mathbf{A}}
, such that
O_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}I^{\bm{\upalpha}}_{\mathbf{B}}=\delta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}
,
I_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}O^{\bm{\upalpha}}_{\mathbf{B}}=\delta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}
, and
O_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}I_{\mathbf{A}}^{\bm{\upbeta}}+I_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}O_{\mathbf{A}}^{\bm{\upbeta}}=\delta_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\bm{\upbeta}}
, while all the other pairings are zero. In particular, we shall express a section of
\mathcal{O}^{\bm{\upalpha}}
as

\displaystyle\Xi^{\bm{\upalpha}}=I^{\bm{\upalpha}}_{\mathbf{A}}\xi^{\mathbf{A}}+O^{\bm{\upalpha}}_{\mathbf{A}}\zeta^{\mathbf{A}},\qquad\text{where}\quad\big(\xi^{\mathbf{A}},\zeta^{\mathbf{A}}\big)\in\mathcal{O}^{\mathbf{A}}+\mathcal{O}^{\mathbf{A}}[-1],

and similarly for dual tractor spinors.

By abuse of notation, the connection on
\mathcal{S}
and the spin connection associated to a metric in the conformal class will both be denoted
\nabla_{a}
. They satisfy

\displaystyle\nabla_{a}O_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bm{\upgamma}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.73624pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.73624pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.59853pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.71323pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{A}}}}_{{{a{\mathbf{B}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}I_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{B}},\qquad

\displaystyle\nabla_{a}I_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathrm{P}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.693pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.35213pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ab}}}\bm{\upgamma}^{{{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{A}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}O_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle\nabla_{a}O^{\bm{\upalpha}}_{\mathbf{A}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bm{\upgamma}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.11264pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.11264pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.85034pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.89308pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{a{\mathbf{A}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}I^{\bm{\upalpha}}_{\mathbf{B}},\qquad

\displaystyle\nabla_{a}I^{\bm{\upalpha}}_{\mathbf{A}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathrm{P}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.693pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.35213pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ab}}}\bm{\upgamma}^{{{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{A}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}O^{\bm{\upalpha}}_{\mathbf{B}},

(2.11)

where
\bm{\upgamma}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{a}{\mathbf{A}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\in\mathcal{O}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{a}{\mathbf{A}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}[1]
satisfy
\bm{\upgamma}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.46266pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.46266pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.49564pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.21117pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{C}}}}_{{{(a}{\mathbf{A}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.06969pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.90692pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\bm{\upgamma}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.64168pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.64168pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00845pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.06969pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.90692pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{b)}{\mathbf{C}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}=-\mathbf{g}_{ab}\delta^{{{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{\mathbf{A}}}}
. The bundle analogue of (
2.9
) is

\displaystyle\Gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{\upbeta}}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}}{\bm{\upalpha}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}=Z_{\mathcal{A}}^{a}\big(O_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}I^{\bm{\upbeta}}_{\mathbf{B}}\bm{\upgamma}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{a}{\mathbf{A}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}-I_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}O^{\bm{\upbeta}}_{\mathbf{B}}\bm{\upgamma}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{a}{\mathbf{A}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\big)+\sqrt{2}Y_{\mathcal{A}}O_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}O^{\bm{\upbeta}}_{\mathbf{A}}-\sqrt{2}X_{\mathcal{A}}I_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}I^{\bm{\upbeta}}_{\mathbf{A}}.

With a choice of conformal scale
\sigma\in\mathcal{O}[1]
for which
g_{ab}=\sigma^{-2}\mathbf{g}_{ab}
is flat, i.e.,
\mathrm{P}_{ab}=0
, equations (
2.11
) can be integrated explicitly to give

\displaystyle I^{\bm{\upalpha}}_{\mathbf{A}}=\mathring{I}^{\bm{\upalpha}}_{\mathbf{A}},\qquad O^{\bm{\upalpha}}_{\mathbf{A}}=\mathring{O}^{\bm{\upalpha}}_{\mathbf{A}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}x^{a}\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.11264pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.11264pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.85034pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.89308pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{a{\mathbf{A}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\mathring{I}^{\bm{\upalpha}}_{\mathbf{B}},\qquad I_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}=\mathring{I}_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}},\qquad O_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}=\mathring{O}_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}x^{a}\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.11264pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.11264pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.85034pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.89308pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{a{\mathbf{A}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\mathring{I}_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{B}},

where
\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.11264pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.11264pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.85034pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.89308pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{a{\mathbf{A}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}=\sigma^{-1}\bm{\upgamma}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.11264pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.11264pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.85034pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.89308pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{a{\mathbf{A}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}
.

Even dimensions.
When
n=2m
, the story is similar, except that, by virtue of the two chiral spinor representations, we have an unprimed tractor spinor bundle and a primed tractor spinor bundle, defined as
\mathcal{S}:=G\times_{P}\mathbb{S}
and
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}:=G\times_{P}\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
respectively. We shall view the generators
\Gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{\upbeta^{\prime}}}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}}{\bm{\upalpha}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3819pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.3819pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.23405pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.44218pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}
and
\Gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.04625pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.04625pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.59703pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.70145pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{\upbeta}}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}}{\bm{\upalpha^{\prime}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}
as holomorphic sections of
\mathcal{T}^{*}\otimes\mathcal{S}^{*}\otimes\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{T}^{*}\otimes(\mathcal{S}^{\prime})^{*}\otimes\mathcal{S}
respectively on
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
, both of which are preserved by the extension of the tractor connection to
\mathcal{S}\oplus\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
.

The spinor spaces
\mathbb{S}
and
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
admit filtrations of
P
-submodules
\mathbb{S}=:\mathbb{S}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\supset\mathbb{S}^{\frac{1}{2}}
and
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}=:{\mathbb{S}^{\prime}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\supset{\mathbb{S}^{\prime}}^{\frac{1}{2}}
. These
P
-modules and their quotients give rise to
P
-invariant vector bundles on
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
in the standard way. The splitting (
2.3
) on
\mathbb{V}
induces a splitting of these filtrations

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}\cong\mathbb{S}_{-\frac{1}{2}}\oplus\mathbb{S}_{\frac{1}{2}},\qquad\mathbb{S}^{\prime}\cong\mathbb{S}_{-\frac{1}{2}}^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{S}_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\prime},

(2.12)

where
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{2}}\cong\mathbb{V}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{S}_{-\frac{1}{2}}
and
\mathbb{S}_{\frac{1}{2}}\cong\mathbb{V}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{S}^{\prime}_{-\frac{1}{2}}
, and we can identify
\mathbb{S}_{-\frac{1}{2}}
and
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}_{-\frac{1}{2}}
, and thus
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{2}}
and
\mathbb{S}_{\frac{1}{2}}
, as the chiral spinor representations of
(\mathbb{V}_{0},g_{ab})
. Elements of
\mathbb{S}_{-\frac{1}{2}}
and
\mathbb{S}_{\frac{1}{2}}
will carry unprimed and primed upper case Roman indices respectively, e.g.,
\eta^{\mathbf{A}}\in\mathbb{S}_{\frac{1}{2}}
and
\xi^{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}}\in\mathbb{S}_{-\frac{1}{2}}
. The generators of the Clifford algebra are matrices denoted
\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B^{\prime}}}}}_{{{a}{\mathbf{A}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.24608pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.24608pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.47093pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.32567pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}
and
\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.24608pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.24608pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.47093pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.32567pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{A}}}}_{{{a}{\mathbf{B^{\prime}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}
, satisfying the Clifford identities

\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.46266pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.46266pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.49564pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.21117pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{C^{\prime}}}}}_{{{(a}{\mathbf{A}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.36693pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.36693pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.53218pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.36942pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.64168pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.64168pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00845pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.36693pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.36693pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.53218pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.36942pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{b)}{\mathbf{C^{\prime}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}=-g_{ab}\delta^{{{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{\mathbf{A}}}}
and
\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.46266pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.46266pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.49564pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.21117pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.62248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.62248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72275pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.50552pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{C}}}}_{{{(a}{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.06969pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.90692pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.64168pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.64168pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00845pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.06969pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.90692pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B^{\prime}}}}}_{{{b)}{\mathbf{C}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.24608pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.24608pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.47093pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.32567pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}=-g_{ab}\delta^{{{\mathbf{B^{\prime}}}}}_{{{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}}}}
, where
\delta^{{{\mathbf{B^{\prime}}}}}_{{{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}}}}
and
\delta^{{{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{\mathbf{A}}}}
are the identity elements on
\mathbb{S}_{-\frac{1}{2}}
and
\mathbb{S}_{\frac{1}{2}}
respectively. We also obtain spin invariant bilinear forms
\gamma^{{{(k)}}}_{{{\mathbf{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}}}}}
,
\gamma^{{{(k)}}}_{{{\mathbf{AB}}}}
and
\gamma^{{{(k)}}}_{{{\mathbf{AB^{\prime}}}}}
. The story for
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
is similar.

We introduce projectors
\mathring{O}_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}
,
\mathring{I}_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}}
and injectors
\mathring{I}^{\bm{\upalpha}}_{\mathbf{A}}
and
\mathring{O}^{\bm{\upalpha}}_{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}}
for the splitting (
2.12
), normalised in the obvious way.
The relation between the generators of the Clifford algebra
{\mathcal{C}\ell}(\mathbb{V},h_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}})
and those of
{\mathcal{C}\ell}(\mathbb{V}_{0},g_{ab})
is then given by

\displaystyle\Gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{\upbeta^{\prime}}}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}}{\bm{\upalpha}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3819pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.3819pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.23405pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.44218pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}=\mathring{Z}_{\mathcal{A}}^{a}\big(\mathring{O}_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathring{I}^{\bm{\upbeta^{\prime}}}_{\mathbf{B^{\prime}}}\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B^{\prime}}}}}_{{{a}{\mathbf{A}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.24608pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.24608pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.47093pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.32567pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}-\mathring{I}_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}}\mathring{O}^{\bm{\upbeta^{\prime}}}_{\mathbf{B}}\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.62248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.62248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72275pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.50552pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{a}{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\big)+\sqrt{2}\mathring{Y}_{\mathcal{A}}\mathring{O}_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathring{O}^{\bm{\upbeta^{\prime}}}_{\mathbf{A}}-\sqrt{2}\mathring{X}_{\mathcal{A}}\mathring{I}_{\bm{\upalpha}}^{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}}\mathring{I}^{\bm{\upbeta^{\prime}}}_{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}},

and similar for
\Gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.04625pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.04625pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.59703pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.70145pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{\upbeta}}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}}{\bm{\upalpha^{\prime}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}
by interchanging primed and unprimed indices.

These algebraic objects extend to weighted tensor or spinor fields just as in odd dimensions in the obvious way and notation. In particular, we have composition series of the unprimed and primed tractor spinor bundles:

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{\bm{\upalpha}}=\mathcal{O}^{\mathbf{A}}+\mathcal{O}^{{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}}}[-1],\qquad

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{{\bm{\upalpha^{\prime}}}}=\mathcal{O}^{{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}}}+\mathcal{O}^{\mathbf{A}}[-1],

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\bm{\upalpha}}=\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}}}[1]+\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{A}},\qquad

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{{\bm{\upalpha^{\prime}}}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{A}}[1]+\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}}}.

2.2
Twistor space

The linear subspaces of
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
can be described in terms of representations of
G=\mathrm{Spin}(n+2,\mathbb{C})
. We shall be interested in those of maximal dimension, arising from maximal totally null vector subspaces of
(\mathbb{V},h_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}})
. In even dimensions, the complex orientation on
\mathbb{V}
determines the duality of the corresponding linear subspaces, via Hodge duality, which are then described as either self-dual or anti-self-dual.

Definition 2.3
.

An
m
-dimensional linear subspace of
\mathcal{Q}^{2m+1}
is called a
\gamma
-plane
. A self-dual, respectively, anti-self-dual,
m
-dimensional linear subspace of
\mathcal{Q}^{2m}
is called an
\alpha
-plane
, respectively, a
\beta
-plane
.

We call the space of all
\gamma
-planes in
\mathcal{Q}^{2m+1}
the
twistor space
of
\mathcal{Q}^{2m+1}
, and denote it by
\mathbb{PT}_{(2m+1)}
. The space of all
\alpha
-planes, respectively,
\beta
-planes in
\mathcal{Q}^{2m}
will be called the
twistor space

\mathbb{PT}_{(2m)}
, respectively, the
primed twistor space

\mathbb{PT}^{\prime}_{(2m)}
.

A point in
\mathbb{PT}
will be referred to as a
twistor
.

We shall often write
\mathbb{PT}
and
\mathbb{PT}^{\prime}
for
\mathbb{PT}_{(2m+1)}
or
\mathbb{PT}_{(2m)}
, and
\mathbb{PT}^{\prime}_{(2m)}
respectively. We now distinguish the odd- and even-dimensional cases.

2.2.1
Odd dimensions

Assume
n=2m+1
. Let
Z^{\bm{\upalpha}}
be a non-zero spinor in
\mathbb{S}
, and define the linear map

\displaystyle Z_{\mathcal{A}}^{\bm{\upalpha}}:=\Gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{\upalpha}}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}}{\bm{\upbeta}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}Z^{\bm{\upbeta}}\colon\ \mathbb{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{S}.

(2.13)

By (
2.1
), the kernel of (
2.13
) is a totally null vector subspace of
\mathbb{V}
, and if it is non-trivial, descends to a linear subspace of
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
.

Definition 2.4
.

We say that a non-zero spinor
Z^{\bm{\upalpha}}
in
\mathbb{S}
is
pure
if the kernel of
Z_{\mathcal{A}}^{\bm{\upalpha}}:=\Gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.90282pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{\upalpha}}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}}{\bm{\upbeta}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}Z^{\bm{\upbeta}}
has maximal dimension
m+1
.

The
(m+1)
-dimensional totally null subspace of
\mathbb{V}
associated in this way to a pure spinor descends to a
\gamma
-plane in
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
. Clearly, any two pure spinors differing by a factor give rise to the same
\gamma
-plane. Further, one can show that any
\gamma
-plane in
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
arises from a pure spinor up to scale. Hence,

Proposition 2.5

(
[
10
]
)
.

The twistor space
\mathbb{PT}
of
\mathcal{Q}^{2m+1}
is isomorphic to the projectivisation of the space of all pure spinors in
\mathbb{S}
.

Every non-zero spinor in
\mathbb{S}
is pure when
m=1
. Let us recall that the
\Gamma^{{{(k)}}}_{{{\bm{\upalpha\upbeta}}}}
in the next theorem denote the spin bilinear forms defined by (
2.2
).

Theorem 2.6

(
[
10
]
)
.

When
m>1
, a non-zero spinor
Z^{\bm{\upalpha}}
in
\mathbb{S}
is pure if and only if it satisfies

\displaystyle\Gamma^{{{(k)}}}_{{{\bm{\upalpha\upbeta}}}}Z^{\bm{\upalpha}}Z^{\bm{\upbeta}}=0,\qquad\text{for all}\quad k<m+1,\quad k\equiv m+2,m+1\pmod{4},

(2.14)

and
\Gamma^{{{(m+1)}}}_{{{\bm{\upalpha\upbeta}}}}Z^{\bm{\upalpha}}Z^{\bm{\upbeta}}\neq 0
.

Alternatively, the quadratic relations (
2.14
) can be expressed more succinctly by
[
35
]

\displaystyle Z^{\mathcal{A}\bm{\upalpha}}Z_{\mathcal{A}}^{\bm{\upbeta}}+Z^{\bm{\upalpha}}Z^{\bm{\upbeta}}=0.

(2.15)

In analogy with the description of the quadric, we shall view
Z^{\bm{\upalpha}}
as a position vector or coordinates on
\mathbb{S}
. The twistor space of
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
can then be described as a complex projective variety of the projectivisation
\mathbb{P}\mathbb{S}
of
\mathbb{S}
with homogeneous coordinates
[Z^{\bm{\upalpha}}]
satisfying (
2.14
) or (
2.15
) when
m>1
. For
\mathcal{Q}^{3}
, we have
\mathbb{PT}_{(3)}\cong\mathbb{CP}^{3}
.

We shall adopt the following notation: if
Z
is a point in
\mathbb{PT}
, with homogeneous coordinates
[Z^{\bm{\upalpha}}]
, then the corresponding
\gamma
-plane in
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
will be denoted
\check{Z}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\check{Z}:=\big\{\big[X^{\mathcal{A}}\big]\in\mathcal{Q}^{n}\colon X^{\mathcal{A}}Z_{\mathcal{A}}^{\bm{\upalpha}}=0\big\}.

Let
\Xi
be a twistor with homogeneous coordinates
[\Xi^{\bm{\upalpha}}]
and associated
\gamma
-plane
\check{\Xi}
in
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
. The projective tangent space of
\mathbb{PT}
at
\Xi
is the linear subspace of
\mathbb{P}\mathbb{S}
defined by

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}_{\Xi}\mathbb{PT}:=\big\{[Z^{{{\bm{\upalpha}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}]\in\mathbb{P}\mathbb{S}\colon\Gamma^{{{(k)}}}_{{{\bm{\upalpha}}{\bm{\upbeta}}}}Z^{{{\bm{\upalpha}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\Xi^{{{\bm{\upbeta}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}=0,\,\text{for all $k<m-1$}\big\}.

(2.16)

This is the closure of the holomorphic tangent space
\mathrm{T}_{\Xi}\mathbb{PT}
at
\Xi
, and contains the linear subspace

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{\Xi}:=\big\{[Z^{{{\bm{\upalpha}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}]\in\mathbb{P}\mathbb{S}\colon\Gamma^{{{(k)}}}_{{{\bm{\upalpha}}{\bm{\upbeta}}}}Z^{{{\bm{\upalpha}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\Xi^{{{\bm{\upbeta}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}=0,\,\text{for all $k<m$}\big\}.

(2.17)

This is the closure of a subspace
\mathrm{D}_{\Xi}
of
\mathrm{T}_{\Xi}\mathbb{PT}
. The smooth assignment of every point
\Xi
of
\mathbb{PT}
of
\mathrm{D}_{\Xi}
yields a distribution that we shall denote
\mathrm{D}
. Another convenient way of expressing the locus in (
2.17
) is
[
35
]
