, and
\widehat{V}_{\underline{\hat{x}}}
its corresponding global holomorphic section of
\mathrm{N}\underline{\hat{x}}
.

•

Suppose
V
is null. When
m=1
,
\widehat{V}_{\underline{\hat{x}}}
has a double zero, which corresponds to the
\gamma
-plane to which
V
is tangent. When
m>1
,
\widehat{V}_{\underline{\hat{x}}}
vanishes on a
\frac{1}{2}m(m-1)
-dimensional algebraic subset of
\underline{\hat{x}}
biholomorphic to
\mathbb{PT}_{(2m-1)}
of multiplicity
2^{m}
. Each point of this subset corresponds to a
\gamma
-plane to which
V
is tangent.

•

Suppose
V
is non-null. When
m=1
,
\widehat{V}_{\underline{\hat{x}}}
has two simple zeros, each of which determines a co-
\gamma
-plane to which
V
is tangent. When
m>1
,
\widehat{V}_{\underline{\hat{x}}}
vanishes on two disjoint
\frac{1}{2}m(m-1)
-dimensional algebraic subsets of
\underline{\hat{x}}
bihomolomorphic to
\mathbb{PT}_{(2m-2)}
and
\mathbb{PT}^{\prime}_{(2m-2)}
, each of multiplicity
2^{m-1}
. Each point of these subsets corresponds to a co-
\gamma
-plane to which
V
is tangent.

Proof.

With no loss, we assume that
x
is the origin in
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{2m+1}\subset\mathcal{Q}^{2m+1}
, and set
V_{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{B}}:=V^{a}\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{a}{\mathbf{A}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}
. To determine the zero set of
\underline{\hat{V}}_{\underline{\hat{x}}}
, we simply remark that
V^{a}\gamma^{{{(m)}}}_{{{aa_{1}\ldots a_{m-1}}{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}}}\pi^{\mathbf{A}}\pi^{\mathbf{B}}=0
is equivalent to the eigenspinor equation
\pi^{\mathbf{C}}V^{{{[\mathbf{A}}}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}}}\pi^{\mathbf{B}]}=0
. We can then proceed as in the proof of Proposition
2.30
according to whether
V^{a}
is null or non-null, and obtain the required zero sets of the section
\widehat{V}_{\underline{\hat{x}}}
in each case, the multiplicities being given by the algebraic multiplicities of the eigenvalues of
V^{{{\mathbf{A}}}}_{{{\mathbf{C}}}}
. In particular, when
m=1
, the solution set is defined by the vanishing of a single homogeneous polynomial of degree
2
, which has two distinct roots generically, but a single root of multiplicity two when
V^{a}
is null – see, e.g.,
[
21
]
.
∎

2.5.3
Even dimensions

The analysis when
n=2m
is very similar to the odd-dimensional case without the added complication of the canonical distribution. Again, for any
x
of
\mathcal{Q}^{2m}
,
\mathrm{T}_{x}\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{2m}
injects into
H^{0}(\hat{x},\mathcal{O}(\mathrm{N}\hat{x}))
. A null vector in
V^{a}
is
\mathrm{T}_{x}\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{2m}
defines a global section
\widehat{V}_{\hat{x}}
of
\mathrm{N}\hat{x}
, which vanishes at a single point when
m=2
, and on a
\frac{1}{2}(m-1)(m-2)
-dimensional algebraic subset of
\hat{x}
, isomorphic to
\mathbb{PT}_{(2m-2)}
, when
m>2
. Each point of this subset corresponds to an
\alpha
-plane to which
V^{a}
is tangent.

2.5.4
Kodaira’s theorem and completeness

Let us now turn to the question of whether
\mathrm{T}_{x}\mathcal{Q}^{n}
maps to
H^{0}(\hat{x},\mathcal{O}(\mathrm{N}\hat{x}))
bijectively, and not merely injectively, for any
x\in\mathcal{Q}^{n}
. By Kodaira’s theorem
[
24
]
,
\mathrm{T}_{x}\mathcal{Q}^{n}\cong H^{0}(\hat{x},\mathcal{O}(\mathrm{N}\hat{x}))\cong\mathbb{C}^{n}
if and only if the family
\{\hat{x}\}
in
\mathbb{PT}
is
complete
, i.e.,
any
infinitesimal deformation of
\hat{x}
arises from an element of
\mathrm{T}_{x}\mathcal{Q}^{n}
. As we have seen in Section
2.2.3
, the twistor space
\mathbb{PT}
of
\mathcal{Q}^{2m+1}
and the twistor space
\widetilde{\mathbb{PT}}
of
\mathcal{Q}^{2m+2}
are both
\frac{1}{2}(m+1)(m+2)
-dimensional complex projective varieties in
\mathbb{CP}^{2^{m+1}-1}
, and it is the embedding
\mathcal{Q}^{2m+1}\subset\mathcal{Q}^{2m+2}
that induces the canonical distribution
{\mathrm{D}}
on
\mathbb{PT}
. The issue here is that Kodaira’s theorem is only concerned with the holomorphic structure of the underlying manifolds, and does not depend on the additional distribution on
\mathbb{PT}
.

Now, by the twistor correspondences, any point
x
in
\mathcal{Q}^{2m+1}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{2m+2}
gives rise to a
\frac{1}{2}m(m+1)
-dimensional complex submanifold
\hat{x}
of
\mathbb{PT}
and
\widetilde{\mathbb{PT}}
respectively. This means that the analytic family
\{\hat{x}\}
parametrised by the points
\{x\}
of
\mathcal{Q}^{2m+1}
can be completed to a larger family parametrised by the points
\{x\}
of
\mathcal{Q}^{2m+2}
via the embedding
\mathcal{Q}^{2m+1}\subset\mathcal{Q}^{2m+2}
. Further, a complex submanifold
\hat{x}
corresponds to a point
x
in
\mathcal{Q}^{2m+1}
if and only if
\hat{x}
is tangent to an
m
-dimensional subspace of
\mathrm{D}_{Z}
at every point
Z\in\hat{x}
.

We also need to check whether the family of
\hat{x}
is complete when
x\in\mathcal{Q}^{2m+2}
. If it were not, one would be able to find a group of biholomorphic automorphisms of
\mathbb{PT}
larger than
\mathrm{Spin}(2m+4,\mathbb{C})
and a parabolic subgroup such that the quotient models
\mathbb{PT}
. But the work of
[
13
,
28
]
tells us that there is no such group. The same applies to each
\hat{x}
, and since these are biholomorphic to flag varieties, the normal bundle
\mathrm{N}\hat{x}
can be identified with a rank-
(m+1)
holomorphic homogeneous vector bundle over
\hat{x}
. In the notation of
[
5
]
, we find that for a point
x
in
\mathcal{Q}^{2m+1}
or
\mathcal{Q}^{2m+2}
, the normal bundle
\mathrm{N}\hat{x}
in
\mathbb{PT}\cong\widetilde{\mathbb{PT}}
is given by

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{cc}m=1&m>1\\
\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.11314pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.11314pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.8889pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\times}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-7.11314pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.8889pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\times}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.11314pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.8889pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\times}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-7.11314pt\raise 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-2.5pt\raise-2.22223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bullet}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 9.9584pt\raise 9.9584pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.8889pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\times}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-2.5pt\raise-2.22223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bullet}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-14.2263pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-2.5pt\raise-2.22223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bullet}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-2.5pt\raise-2.22223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bullet}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 9.9584pt\raise 9.9584pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.8889pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\times}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 9.9584pt\raise-9.9584pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-2.5pt\raise-2.22223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bullet}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-28.4526pt\raise 5.69052pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-1.70142pt\raise-1.61111pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptscriptstyle{1}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-14.2263pt\raise 5.69052pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-1.70142pt\raise-1.61111pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 5.69052pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-1.70142pt\raise-1.61111pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.9584pt\raise 15.64893pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-1.70142pt\raise-1.61111pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.9584pt\raise-4.26788pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-1.70142pt\raise-1.61111pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}$}}}}}}}}}}_{\mbox{\tiny{$m+1$ nodes}}}\end{array}

Here, the mutilated Dynkin diagram corresponds to the parabolic subalgebra underlying the flag variety
\hat{x}
, and the coefficients over the nodes to the irreducible representation that determines the vector bundle. When
m=1
, i.e., for
\mathcal{Q}^{3}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{4}
, we recover the well-known result
\mathrm{N}_{\hat{x}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\hat{x}}(1)\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\hat{x}}(1)
, where
\mathcal{O}_{\hat{x}}(1)
is the hyperplane bundle over
\hat{x}\cong\mathbb{CP}^{1}
. We can compute the cohomology using the Bott–Borel–Weil theorem, and verify that indeed
H^{0}(\hat{x},\mathcal{O}(\mathrm{N}\hat{x}))\cong\mathbb{C}^{2m+2}
and
H^{1}(\hat{x},\mathcal{O}(\mathrm{N}\hat{x}))=0
– this latter condition tells us that there is no obstruction for the existence of our family.

We can play the same game with the family of compact complex submanifolds
\{\underline{\hat{x}}\}
in
\mathbb{M}\mathbb{T}
parametrised by the points
x
of
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{2m+1}
. But in this case, for any
x
of
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{2m+1}
, the normal bundle
\mathrm{N}\underline{\hat{x}}
is essentially the total space of
\mathrm{T}^{-1}\hat{x}\rightarrow\hat{x}
, and is described, in the notation of
[
5
]
, as the rank-
m
holomorphic homogeneous vector bundle

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{cc}m=1&m>1\\
\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.8889pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\times}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 5.69052pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-1.70142pt\raise-1.61111pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptscriptstyle{2}}$}}}}}}}}}&\underbrace{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 28.4526pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-28.4526pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-2.5pt\raise-2.22223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bullet}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-14.2263pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-2.5pt\raise-2.22223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bullet}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-2.5pt\raise-2.22223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bullet}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 14.2263pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.8889pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\times}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-28.4526pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-2.5pt\raise-2.22223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bullet}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-14.2263pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-2.5pt\raise-2.22223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bullet}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-2.5pt\raise-2.22223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bullet}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 14.2263pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.8889pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\times}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-28.4526pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-2.5pt\raise-2.22223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bullet}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-14.2263pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-2.5pt\raise-2.22223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bullet}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-2.5pt\raise-2.22223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bullet}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 14.2263pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.8889pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\times}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-28.4526pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-2.5pt\raise-2.22223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bullet}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-14.2263pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-2.5pt\raise-2.22223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bullet}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-2.5pt\raise-2.22223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bullet}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 14.2263pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.8889pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\times}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.11314pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.8889pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{>}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-28.4526pt\raise 5.69052pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-1.70142pt\raise-1.61111pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptscriptstyle{1}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-14.2263pt\raise 5.69052pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-1.70142pt\raise-1.61111pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 5.69052pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-1.70142pt\raise-1.61111pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.2263pt\raise 5.69052pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-1.70142pt\raise-1.61111pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}$}}}}}}}}}}_{\mbox{\tiny{$m$ nodes}}}\end{array}

When
m=1
, i.e.,
\mathcal{Q}^{3}
,
\underline{\hat{x}}\cong\mathbb{CP}^{1}
, and we recover the well-known result
\mathcal{O}(\mathrm{N}{\underline{\hat{x}}})\cong\mathcal{O}_{\underline{\hat{x}}}(2):=\otimes^{2}\mathcal{O}_{\underline{\hat{x}}}(1)
. Again, the Bott–Borel–Weil theorem confirms that
H^{0}(\underline{\hat{x}},\mathcal{O}(\mathrm{N}\underline{\hat{x}}))\cong\mathbb{C}^{2m+1}
and
H^{1}(\underline{\hat{x}},\mathcal{O}(\underline{\mathrm{N}\hat{x}}))=0
.

Remark 2.32
.

When
n=3
, this analysis was already exploited in
[
25
]
in the curved setting, where the twistor space of a three-dimensional holomorphic conformal structure is identified with the space of null geodesics. See also
[
18
]
.

3
Null foliations

As before, we work in the holomorphic category throughout, i.e., vector fields and distributions will be assumed to be holomorphic.

Definition 3.1
.

An
almost null structure
is a holomorphic totally null
m
-plane distribution on
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
, where
n=2m
or
2m+1
.

In other words, an almost null structure is a
\gamma
-plane,
\alpha
-plane or
\beta
-plane distribution. From the discussion of Section
2.3
, an almost null structure, self-dual when
n=2m
, can be viewed as a holomorphic section of
\mathbb{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{Q}^{n}
, or equivalently as a projective pure spinor field on
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
, that is a spinor field defined up to scale, and which is pure at every point. The geometric properties of an almost null structure on a general spin complex Riemannian manifold can be expressed in terms of the differential properties of its corresponding projective pure spinor field as described in
[
34
,
35
]
.

The question we now wish to address is the following one: given an almost null structure, how can we encode its geometric properties in twistor space
\mathbb{PT}
?

3.1
Odd dimensions

When
n=2m+1
, an almost null structure is more adequately expressed as an inclusion of distributions
N\subset N^{\perp}
where
N
is a holomorphic totally null
m
-plane distribution and
N^{\perp}
is its orthogonal complement. One can then investigate the geometric properties of
N
and
N^{\perp}
independently. In the following,
\Gamma(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{O}(N))
denotes the space of holomorphic sections of
N
over an open subset
\mathcal{U}
of
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
, and similarly for
N^{\perp}
.

Definition 3.2
.

Let
N\subset N^{\perp}
be an almost null structure on some open subset
\mathcal{U}
of
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
. We say that
N
is

•

integrable
if
[\bm{X},\bm{Y}]\in\Gamma(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{O}(N))
for all
\bm{X},\bm{Y}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{O}(N))
,

•

totally geodetic
if
\nabla_{\bm{Y}}\bm{X}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{O}(N))
for all
\bm{X},\bm{Y}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{O}(N))
,

•

co-integrable
if
[\bm{X},\bm{Y}]\in\Gamma(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{O}(N^{\perp}))
for all
\bm{X},\bm{Y}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{O}(N^{\perp}))
,

•

totally co-geodetic
if
\nabla_{\bm{Y}}\bm{X}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{O}(N^{\perp}))
for all
\bm{X},\bm{Y}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{O}(N^{\perp}))
.

An integrable almost null structure will be referred to as a
null structure
.

There is however some dependency regarding the geometric properties of
N
and
N^{\perp}
.

Lemma 3.3

(
[
35
]
)
.

Let
N
be an almost null structure. Then

•

if
N
is totally co-geodetic, it is also integrable and co-integrable,

•

if
N
is integrable and co-integrable, it is also geodetic,

•

if
N
is totally geodetic, it is also integrable.

Another important point is the conformal invariance of the above properties. All with the exception of the totally co-geodetic property are conformal invariant – see
[
35
]
.

3.1.1
Local description

The next theorems will be local in nature. That means that we shall work on
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}
viewed as a dense open subset of
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
. For their proofs, we shall make use of the local coordinates on
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}
,
\mathbb{F}_{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}}
and
\mathbb{PT}_{\setminus\widehat{\infty}}
given in Appendix
A.1
. Let
N
be an almost null structure on some open subset
\mathcal{U}
of
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}=\{z^{A},z_{A},u\}
, and view
N
as a local holomorphic section of
\mathbb{F}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}
, i.e., a holomorphic projective pure spinor field
[\xi^{\mathbf{A}}]
. We may assume that locally,
[\xi^{\mathbf{A}}]
defines a complex submanifold of
\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{U}_{0}
, where
(\mathcal{U}_{0},(\pi^{A},\pi^{AB}))
is a coordinate chart on the fibers of
\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{U}}
, given by the graph

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\xi}:=\big\{(x,\pi)\in\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{U}_{0}\colon\pi^{AB}=\xi^{AB}(x),\,\pi^{A}=\xi^{A}(x)\big\},

(3.1)

for some
\frac{1}{2}m(m-1)
and
m
holomorphic functions
\xi^{AB}=\xi^{[AB]}
and
\xi^{A}
respectively on
\mathcal{U}
. In this case, the distribution
N
is spanned by the
m
holomorphic vector fields

\displaystyle\bm{Z}^{A}=\partial^{A}+\left(\xi^{AD}-\tfrac{1}{2}\xi^{A}\xi^{D}\right)\partial_{D}+\xi^{A}\partial,

(3.2)

while its orthogonal complement
N^{\perp}
by the
m+1
holomorphic vector fields

\displaystyle\bm{Z}^{A}=\partial^{A}+\left(\xi^{AD}-\tfrac{1}{2}\xi^{A}\xi^{D}\right)\partial_{D}+\xi^{A}\partial,\qquad\bm{U}=\partial-\xi^{D}\partial_{D},

(3.3)

where
\partial^{A}:=\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{A}}
,
\partial_{A}:=\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{A}}
and
\partial:=\frac{\partial}{\partial u}
. Here, we shall make a slight abuse of notation by denoting the vector fields spanning
N
and
N^{\perp}
, and their lifts to
\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{U}}
, both by (
3.2
) and (
3.3
).

Remark 3.4
.

It will be understood that when
m=1
there are no coordinates
\pi^{AB}
. This does not affect the veracity of the following results in this case – see however Remark
3.7
.

3.1.2
Totally geodetic null structures

Let
\mathcal{W}
be an
(m+1)
-dimensional complex submanifold of
\mathbb{PT}
and let
\mathcal{U}
be an open subset of
\mathcal{Q}^{2m+1}
. Suppose that for every point
x
of
\mathcal{U}
,
\hat{x}\in\widehat{\mathcal{U}}
intersects
\mathcal{W}
transversely in a point. Then each point of
\mathcal{W}\cap\hat{x}
determines a point in the fiber
\mathbb{F}_{x}
, and thus a
\gamma
-plane through
x
. Smooth variations of the point
x
in
\mathcal{U}
thus define a holomorphic section of
\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{U}}\rightarrow\mathcal{U}
and an
(m+1)
-dimensional analytic family of
\gamma
-planes, each of which being the totally geodetic leaf of an integrable almost null structure. Conversely, consider a local foliation by totally null and totally geodetic
m
-dimensional leaves. Then, each leaf must be some affine subset of a
\gamma
-plane. The
(m+1)
-dimensional leaf space of the foliation constitutes an
(m+1)
-dimensional analytic family of
\gamma
-planes, and thus defines an
(m+1)
-dimensional complex submanifold of
\mathbb{PT}
.

Theorem 3.5
.

A totally geodetic null structure on some open subset
\mathcal{U}
of
\mathcal{Q}^{2m+1}
gives rise to an
(m+1)
-dimensional complex submanifold of
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}\subset\mathbb{PT}
intersecting
\hat{x}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{U}}
transversely for each
x\in\mathcal{U}
. Conversely, any totally geodetic null structure locally arises in this way.

Proof.

Let
N
be an almost null structure as described in Section
3.1.1
. The condition that
N
be totally geodetic is
\bm{g}(\nabla_{\bm{Z}^{A}}\bm{Z}^{B},\bm{Z}^{C})=\bm{g}(\nabla_{\bm{Z}^{A}}\bm{Z}^{B},\bm{U})=0
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left(\partial^{A}+\left(\xi^{AD}-\tfrac{1}{2}\xi^{A}\xi^{D}\right)\partial_{D}+\xi^{A}\partial\right)\xi^{BC}=0,\\
&\left(\partial^{A}+\left(\xi^{AD}-\tfrac{1}{2}\xi^{A}\xi^{D}\right)\partial_{D}+\xi^{A}\partial\right)\xi^{B}=0.\end{split}

(3.4)

We re-express the system (
3.4
) of holomorphic partial differential equations as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\rho^{ABC}+\left(\pi^{AD}-\tfrac{1}{2}\pi^{A}\pi^{D}\right)\rho_{D}^{BC}+\pi^{A}\rho^{BC}=0,\\
&\sigma^{AB}+\left(\pi^{AD}-\tfrac{1}{2}\pi^{A}\pi^{D}\right)\sigma_{D}^{B}+\pi^{A}\sigma^{B}=0,\end{split}

(3.5)

where
\rho^{ABC}:=\partial^{A}\pi^{BC}
,
\rho_{A}^{BC}:=\partial_{A}\pi^{BC}
,
\rho^{AB}:=\partial\pi^{AB}
,
\sigma^{AB}:=\partial^{A}\pi^{B}
,
\sigma_{A}^{B}:=\partial_{A}\pi^{B}
,
\sigma^{A}:=\partial\pi^{A}
. In the language of jets, the locus (
3.5
) defines a complex submanifold of the first jet space
\mathcal{J}^{1}(\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n},\mathcal{U}_{0})
, of which the prolongation of the section
\Gamma_{\xi}
is a submanifold. Now, the distribution
\mathrm{T}^{-1}_{E}\mathbb{F}=\langle\bm{Z}^{A}\rangle
tangent to the fibers of
\mathbb{F}\rightarrow\mathbb{PT}
is annihilated by the
1
-forms
\mathrm{d}\pi^{A}
,
\mathrm{d}\pi^{AB}
,
\bm{\theta}^{A}
and
\bm{\theta}^{0}
as defined in Appendix
A.1
, which can be pulled back to
\mathcal{J}^{1}(\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n},\mathcal{U}_{0})
. The
1
-forms defined by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bm{\phi}^{A}:=\mathrm{d}\pi^{A}-\sigma_{C}^{A}\bm{\theta}^{C}-\left(\sigma^{A}-\sigma_{C}^{A}\pi^{C}\right)\bm{\theta}^{0},\\
&\bm{\phi}^{AB}:=\mathrm{d}\pi^{AB}-\rho_{C}^{AB}\bm{\theta}^{C}-\left(\rho^{AB}-\rho_{C}^{AB}\pi^{C}\right)\bm{\theta}^{0},\end{split}

(3.6)

vanish on the locus (
3.5
), and this implies in particular that, for generic
\rho_{C}^{AB}
,
\rho^{BC}
,
\rho_{C}^{A}
,
\rho^{C}
, the section
\Gamma_{\xi}
must be constant along the fibers of
\mathrm{T}^{-1}_{E}\mathbb{F}
, i.e., the functions
(\xi^{A},\xi^{AB})
depend only on the coordinates
(\omega^{0},\omega^{A},\pi^{A},\pi^{AB})
of the chart
\mathcal{V}_{0}
of
\mathbb{PT}
. Thus, quotienting
\Gamma_{\xi}
along the fibers of
\mathbb{F}\rightarrow\mathbb{PT}
yields an
(m+1)
-dimensional complex submanifold of
\mathbb{PT}
intersecting each
\hat{x}
transversely in a point.

The converse is also true: we start with an
(m+1)
-dimensional complex submanifold
\mathcal{W}
, say, of
\mathbb{PT}
, which can be locally represented by the vanishing of
\frac{1}{2}m(m+1)
holomorphic functions
(F^{AB},F^{A})
on the chart
(\mathcal{V}_{0},(\omega^{0},\omega^{A},\pi^{A},\pi^{AB}))
. Then
(\mathrm{d}F^{AB},\mathrm{d}F^{A})
are a set of
1
-forms vanishing on
\mathcal{W}
. We shall assume that for each
x\in\mathcal{U}
, the submanifold
\hat{x}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{U}}
intersects
\mathcal{W}
transversely in a point. This singles out a local holomorphic section
[\xi^{\mathbf{A}}]
of
\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{U}_{0}\subset\mathbb{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{U}
. By the implicit function theorem, we may assume with no loss of generality that this is the graph
\Gamma_{\xi}
given by (
3.1
). The pullbacks of
(\mathrm{d}F^{AB},\mathrm{d}F^{A})
to
\mathbb{F}
vanish on
\Gamma_{\xi}
and give the restriction

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}Q_{C}^{A}&Q_{CD}^{A}\vskip 2.84526pt\\
Q_{C}^{AB}&Q_{CD}^{AB}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{d}\pi^{C}\\
\mathrm{d}\pi^{CD}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}\bm{Y}F^{A}&\bm{X}_{C}F^{A}\\
\bm{Y}F^{AB}&\bm{X}_{C}F^{AB}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\bm{\theta}^{0}\\
\bm{\theta}^{C}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
0\end{pmatrix},

(3.7)

where

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}Q_{C}^{A}&Q_{CD}^{A}\vskip 2.84526pt\\
Q_{C}^{AB}&Q_{CD}^{AB}\end{pmatrix}:=\begin{pmatrix}\displaystyle\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial\pi^{C}}+\frac{1}{2}u\frac{\partial}{\partial\omega^{C}}-z_{C}\frac{\partial}{\partial\omega^{0}}\right)F^{A}\!\!&\displaystyle\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial\pi^{CD}}+z_{[C}\frac{\partial}{\partial\omega^{D]}}\right)F^{A}\vskip 2.84526pt\\
\displaystyle\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial\pi^{C}}+\frac{1}{2}u\frac{\partial}{\partial\omega^{C}}-z_{C}\frac{\partial}{\partial\omega^{0}}\right)F^{AB}\!\!&\displaystyle\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial\pi^{CD}}+z_{[C}\frac{\partial}{\partial\omega^{D]}}\right)F^{AB}\end{pmatrix}\!.\!\!

(3.8)

At generic points, the matrix (
3.8
) is invertible, and equations (
3.7
) can immediately be seen to be equivalent to the vanishing of the forms (
3.6
). In particular,
\pi^{AB}=\xi^{AB}(x)
and
\pi^{A}=\xi^{A}(x)
satisfy (
3.4
), i.e., the distribution associated to the graph
\Gamma_{\xi}
is integrable and totally geodetic.
∎

3.1.3
Co-integrable null structures

Let us now suppose that our almost null structure
N
is integrable and co-integrable on
\mathcal{U}
. We then have two foliations of
\mathcal{U}
, one for
N
and the other for
N^{\perp}
. By Lemma
3.3
, we know that each leaf of
N
is totally geodetic and therefore a
\gamma
-plane. Since
N\subset N^{\perp}
, each
(m+1)
-dimensional leaf of
N^{\perp}
contains a one-parameter holomorphic family
\{\check{Z}_{t}\}
of
\gamma
-planes, i.e., of leaves of
N
. Thus each leaf of
N^{\perp}
descends to a holomorphic curve on the leaf space of
N
. In particular, by Theorem
3.5
, we can identify the leaf space of
N
with an
(m+1)
-dimensional complex submanifold
\mathcal{W}
of
\mathbb{PT}
foliated by curves, each of which being a one-parameter of twistors
\{Z_{t}\}
and, as we shall show, tangent to the canonical distribution
\mathrm{D}
of
\mathbb{PT}
.

We start by the remark that at any point
Z
of
\mathcal{W}
, any submanifold
\hat{x}
intersects
\mathcal{W}
transversely, i.e.,
\mathrm{T}_{Z}\mathbb{PT}=\mathrm{T}_{Z}\hat{x}\oplus\mathrm{T}_{Z}\mathcal{W}
. Hence, by Lemma
2.27
the intersection of
{\mathrm{D}}_{Z}
with
\mathrm{T}_{Z}\mathcal{W}
can only be at most one-dimensional. Now, let
Z_{0}
and
Z_{t}
be two points on
\mathcal{W}
corresponding to two infinitesimally separated
\gamma
-planes,
\check{Z}_{0}
and
\check{Z}_{t}
in
\{\check{Z}_{t}\}
, contained in the co-
\gamma
-plane
\check{Z}^{\perp}_{0}
. Let
x
and
y
be points on
\check{Z}_{0}
and
\check{Z}_{t}
respectively, so that their corresponding complex submanifolds
\hat{x}
and
\hat{y}
of
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}
intersect
\mathcal{W}
in
Z_{0}
and
Z_{t}
respectively. The vector
V^{a}
in
\mathrm{T}_{x}\mathcal{U}
tangent to
\check{Z}^{\perp}_{0}
connecting
x
to
y
is non-null, and we know by Lemma
2.29
that the vector connecting
Z_{0}
to
Z_{t}
must lie in
\mathrm{D}_{Z_{0}}
. This is clearly independent of the choice of points
x
and
y
on
\check{Z}_{0}
and
\check{Z}_{t}
. Assigning a vector tangent to
{\mathrm{D}}_{Z_{t}}
at every point of
\{Z_{t}\}
yields a curve corresponding to a leaf of
N^{\perp}
. Proceeding in this way for each leaf of
N^{\perp}
gives rise to a foliation by holomorphic curves tangent to
{\mathrm{D}}
on
\mathcal{W}
. Conversely, any such foliation by curves on a given
(m+1)
-submanifold of
\mathbb{PT}
gives rise to an integrable and co-integrable almost null structure.

Theorem 3.6
.

An integrable and co-integrable almost null structure on some open subset
\mathcal{U}
of
\mathcal{Q}^{2m+1}
gives rise to an
(m+1)
-dimensional complex submanifold of
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}\subset\mathbb{PT}
foliated by holomorphic curves tangent to
\mathrm{D}
and intersecting
\hat{x}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{U}}
transversely for each
x\in\mathcal{U}
. Conversely, any integrable and co-integrable almost null structure locally arises in this way.

Proof.

We recycle the setting and notation of the proof of Theorem
3.5
. In particular, we take
N
and
N^{\perp}
to be spanned by the vector fields (
3.2
) and (
3.3
). The assumption that
N
be integrable and co-integrable, i.e.,
\bm{g}(\nabla_{\bm{Z}^{A}}\bm{Z}^{B},\bm{Z}^{C})=\bm{g}(\nabla_{\bm{Z}^{A}}\bm{Z}^{B},\bm{U})=\bm{g}(\nabla_{\bm{U}}\bm{Z}^{B},\bm{Z}^{C})=0
, gives (
3.4
) and in addition,

\displaystyle\big(\partial-\xi^{D}\partial_{D}\big)\xi^{BC}+\big(\big(\partial-\xi^{D}\partial_{D}\big)\xi^{[B}\big)\xi^{C]}=0.

(3.9)

Thus, the system {(
3.4
), (
3.9
)} can be encoded as the complex submanifold of
\mathcal{J}^{1}(\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n},\mathcal{U}_{0})
arising from the intersection of the locus (
3.5
) and the locus

\displaystyle\rho^{BC}-\pi^{D}\rho_{D}^{BC}+\sigma^{[B}\pi^{C]}-\pi^{D}\sigma_{D}^{[B}\pi^{C]}=0,

(3.10)

and the prolongation of
\Gamma_{\xi}
must lie in this intersection. Now, let us define
\bm{\psi}^{AB}:=\bm{\phi}^{AB}-\pi^{[A}\bm{\phi}^{B]}
, where
\bm{\phi}^{A}
and
\bm{\phi}^{AB}
are the
1
-forms (
3.6
). From the proof of Theorem
3.5
, the
1
-forms
\bm{\psi}^{AB}
and
\bm{\phi}^{A}
vanish on the locus (
3.5
). On the other hand, on restriction to the locus (
3.10
), we have
\bm{\psi}^{AB}=\bm{\alpha}^{AB}-\left(\rho_{C}^{AB}-\pi^{[A}\sigma_{C}^{B]}\right)\bm{\theta}^{C}
, where
\langle\bm{\alpha}^{AB},\bm{\theta}^{A}\rangle
annihilate the rank-
(2m+1)
distribution
\mathrm{T}^{-2}_{E}\mathbb{F}=\left\langle\bm{U},\bm{W}_{A},\bm{Z}^{A}\right\rangle
. One can further check that
\langle\bm{\psi}^{AB},\bm{\phi}^{A}\rangle
annihilate the
m+1
vector fields
\bm{U}+\left(\sigma^{A}-\sigma_{B}^{A}\pi^{B}\right)\bm{W}_{A}
and
\bm{Z}^{A}
. These span a rank-
(m+1)
subdistribution
\mathrm{L}
, say, of
\mathrm{T}^{-2}_{E}\mathbb{F}
tangent to
\Gamma_{\xi}
. By Theorem
3.5
,
\Gamma_{\xi}
descends to an
(m+1)
-dimensional complex submanifold
\mathcal{W}
of
\mathbb{PT}
. The quotient
\mathrm{L}/\mathrm{T}^{-1}_{E}\mathbb{F}
is a rank-
1
subbundle of
\mathrm{T}^{-2}_{E}\mathbb{F}/\mathrm{T}^{-1}_{E}\mathbb{F}
, which also descends to a rank-
1
subdistribution of
{\mathrm{D}}=\mathrm{T}^{-1}\mathbb{PT}
tangent to
\mathcal{W}
. This proves the first part of the theorem.

Conversely, consider a complex submanifold
\mathcal{W}
of
\mathbb{PT}
, transverse to every
\hat{x}
in
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}
, given by the vanishing of holomorphic functions
(F^{AB},F^{A})
on the chart
(\mathcal{V}_{0},(\omega^{0},\omega^{A},\pi^{A},\pi^{AB}))
. By Theorem
3.5
, we can associate to
\mathcal{W}
a local section
[\xi^{\mathbf{A}}]
of
\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{U}_{0}\subset\mathbb{F}
with graph
\Gamma_{\xi}
, so that equations (
3.5
) hold. Assume further that the intersection of
\mathrm{T}\mathcal{W}
and
\left.\mathrm{D}\right|_{\mathcal{W}}
is one-dimensional at every point. Then the pullbacks of
(\mathrm{d}F^{AB},\mathrm{d}F^{A})
to
\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{U}_{0}\subset\mathbb{F}
must vanish on
\Gamma_{\xi}
and annihilate both
\mathrm{T}^{-1}_{E}\mathbb{F}
and a rank-
(m+1)
subbundle of
\mathrm{T}^{-2}_{E}\mathbb{F}\supset\mathrm{T}^{-1}_{E}\mathbb{F}
. Thus, there exists a vector field
\bm{V}=\bm{U}+V^{A}\bm{W}_{A}
, for some holomorphic functions
V^{A}
on
\Gamma_{\xi}
, annihilating the
1
-forms (
3.6
). It is then straightforward to check that this gives us precisely the additional restrictions (
3.10
). In particular,
\pi^{AB}=\xi^{AB}(x)
and
\pi^{A}=\xi^{A}(x)
satisfy (
3.4
) and (
3.9
), i.e., the distribution associated to the graph
\Gamma_{\xi}
is integrable and co-integrable.
∎

Remark 3.7
.

When
n=3
, Theorems
3.5
and
3.6
are equivalent: since
\mathbb{PT}
is
3
-dimensional and
\mathrm{D}
has rank
2
, any
2
-dimensional complex submanifold of
\mathbb{PT}
satisfying the transversality property of the theorems must have non-trivial intersection with
\mathrm{D}
.

3.1.4
Totally co-geodetic null structures

Finally, we consider a totally co-geodetic null structure
N
. The key point here is that this stronger requirement is not conformally invariant, and for this reason, the appropriate arena is the mini-twistor space
\mathbb{M}\mathbb{T}
of
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{2m+1}
. In this case, each leaf of the foliation of
N^{\perp}
is totally geodetic, and must therefore be a co-
\gamma
-plane. The
m
-dimensional leaf space can then be identified as an
m
-dimensional complex submanifold
\underline{\mathcal{W}}
of
\mathbb{M}\mathbb{T}
.

Alternatively, we can recycle the setting of Theorems
3.5
and
3.6
: since
N
is in particular integrable and co-integrable, its leaf space is an
(m+1)
-dimensional complex submanifold
\mathcal{W}
of
\mathbb{PT}_{\setminus\widehat{\infty}}
foliated by curves. However, these curves are very particular since they correspond to totally geodetic leaves of
N^{\perp}
. Breaking of the conformal invariance can be translated into these curves being the integral curves of the vector field
\bm{Y}
induced by the point
\infty
on
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
. Quotienting the submanifold
\mathcal{W}
by the flow of
\bm{Y}
thus yields an
m
-dimensional complex submanifold
\underline{\mathcal{W}}
of
\mathbb{M}\mathbb{T}
.

Theorem 3.8
.

A totally co-geodetic null structure on some open subset
\mathcal{U}
of
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{2m+1}
gives rise to an
m
-dimensional complex submanifold of
\underline{\widehat{\mathcal{U}}}\subset\mathbb{M}\mathbb{T}
intersecting each
\hat{x}\subset\underline{\widehat{\mathcal{U}}}
transversely for each
x\in\mathcal{U}
. Conversely, any totally co-geodetic null structure locally arises in this way.

Proof.

Suppose
N
and
N^{\perp}
are both integrable as in the previous section. As already pointed out the integral manifolds of
N
are totally geodetic. We now impose the further assumption that the integral manifolds of
N^{\perp}
are also totally geodetic on
\mathcal{U}
, i.e.,
\bm{g}(\nabla_{\bm{Z}^{A}}\bm{Z}^{B},\bm{Z}^{C})=\bm{g}(\nabla_{\bm{Z}^{A}}\bm{Z}^{B},\bm{U})=\bm{g}(\nabla_{\bm{U}}\bm{Z}^{B},\bm{Z}^{C})=\bm{g}(\nabla_{\bm{U}}\bm{Z}^{A},\bm{U})=0
. Then, in addition to (
3.4
), we have

\displaystyle\big(\partial-\xi^{D}\partial_{D}\big)\xi^{AB}=0,\qquad\big(\partial-\xi^{D}\partial_{D}\big)\xi^{A}=0,

(3.11)

which can be seen to imply (
3.9
). As before, using the same notation as in the proof of Theorem
3.5
, we express the system (
3.4
), (
3.11
) as a complex submanifold of
\mathcal{J}^{1}(\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n},\mathcal{U}_{0})
defined by (
3.5
) and

\displaystyle\rho^{AB}-\pi^{D}\rho_{D}^{AB}=0,\qquad\sigma^{A}-\pi^{D}\sigma_{D}^{A}=0.

(3.12)

In particular, the
1
-forms
\mathrm{d}\pi^{AB}-\rho_{C}^{AB}\bm{\theta}^{C}
and
\mathrm{d}\pi^{A}-\sigma_{C}^{A}\bm{\theta}^{C}
vanish on the locus (
3.5
) and (
3.12
), and this implies in particular that, for generic
\rho_{C}^{AB}
,
\rho^{BC}
,
\rho_{C}^{A}
,
\rho^{C}
, the section
\Gamma_{\xi}
must be constant along the fibers of
\mathbb{F}\rightarrow\mathbb{M}\mathbb{T}
, i.e., the functions
(\xi^{A},\xi^{AB})
depend only on the coordinates
(\underline{\omega}^{A},\pi^{A},\pi^{AB})
on the chart
\underline{\mathcal{V}}_{0}
of
\mathbb{M}\mathbb{T}
. Thus, quotienting
\Gamma_{\xi}
along the fibers of
\mathbb{F}\rightarrow\mathbb{M}\mathbb{T}
yields an
m
-dimensional complex submanifold of
\mathbb{M}\mathbb{T}
.

For the converse, we simply run the argument backwards as in the proof of Theorem
3.5
.
∎

3.2
Even dimensions

The even-dimensional case is somewhat more tractable than the odd-dimensional case. For one, the orthogonal complement of an
\alpha
-plane or
\beta
-plane distribution
N

is

N
itself, i.e.,
N^{\perp}=N
. Definition
3.2
still applies albeit with much redundancy. In particular,
N
is integrable if and only if it is co-integrable. The question now reduces to whether
N
is integrable or not, and if so, whether it is totally geodetic. But it turns out that these two questions are equivalent.

Lemma 3.9
.

An almost null structure is integrable if and only if it is totally geodetic.

For a proof, see for instance
[
33
,
34
]
. The argument leading up to Theorem
3.5
equally applies to the even-dimensional case – simply substitute
\gamma
-plane for
\alpha
-plane. For the sake of completeness, we restate the theorem, which was first used in four dimensions in
[
23
]
, reformulated in twistor language in
[
29
]
, and generalised to higher even dimensions in
[
20
]
. The proof of Theorem
3.5
can be recycled entirely by ‘switching off’ the coordinates
u
,
\omega^{0}
,
\pi^{A}
, and so on.

Theorem 3.10

(
[
20
]
)
.

A self-dual null structure on some open subset
\mathcal{U}
of
\mathcal{Q}^{2m}
gives rise to an
m
-dimensional complex submanifold of
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}\subset\mathbb{PT}
intersecting
\hat{x}
in
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}
transversely for each
x\in\mathcal{U}
. Conversely, any self-dual null structure locally arises in this way.

4
Examples

We now give two examples of co-integrable null structures that will illustrate the mechanism of Theorems
3.6
and
3.10
. These arise in connections with conformal Killing spinors and conformal Killing–Yano
2
-forms, and are more transparently constructed in the language of tractor bundles reviewed in Section
2.1.1
. As before, we work in the holomorphic category.

4.1
Conformal Killing spinors

For definiteness, let us stick to odd dimensions, i.e.,
n=2m+1
. The even-dimensional case is similar. A (holomorphic)
conformal Killing spinor
on
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
is a section
\xi^{\mathbf{A}}
of
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbf{A}}
that satisfies

\displaystyle\nabla_{a}\xi^{\mathbf{A}}+\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bm{\upgamma}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{A}}}}_{{{a}{\mathbf{B}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\zeta^{\mathbf{B}}=0,

(4.1)

where
\zeta^{\mathbf{A}}=\frac{\sqrt{2}}{n}\bm{\upgamma}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{A}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\nabla_{a}\xi^{\mathbf{B}}
is a section of
\mathcal{O}^{\mathbf{A}}[-1]
.

The prolongation of equation (
4.1
) is given by (see for instance
[
6
]
and references therein)

\displaystyle\nabla_{a}\xi^{\mathbf{A}}+\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bm{\upgamma}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{A}}}}_{{{a}{\mathbf{B}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\zeta^{\mathbf{B}}=0,\qquad\nabla_{a}\zeta^{\mathbf{A}}+\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathrm{P}_{ab}\bm{\upgamma}^{{{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{A}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\xi^{\mathbf{B}}=0.

(4.2)

These equations are equivalent to the tractor spinor
\Xi^{\bm{\upalpha}}=(\xi^{\mathbf{A}},\zeta^{\mathbf{A}})
being parallel with respect to the tractor spinor connection, i.e.,
\nabla_{a}\Xi^{\bm{\upalpha}}=0
. In a conformal scale for which the metric is flat, integration of (
4.2
) yields

\displaystyle\xi^{\mathbf{A}}=\mathring{\xi}^{\mathbf{A}}-\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}x^{a}\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{A}}}}_{{{a}{\mathbf{B}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\mathring{\zeta}^{\mathbf{B}},\qquad\zeta^{\mathbf{A}}=\mathring{\zeta}^{\mathbf{A}},

(4.3)

where
\mathring{\xi}^{\mathbf{A}}
and
\mathring{\zeta}^{\mathbf{A}}
denote the constants of integrations at the origin.

A
pure
conformal Killing spinor
\xi^{\mathbf{A}}
defines an almost null structure. The following proposition combines results from
[
34
,
35
]
recast in the language of tractors using Lemmata
2.19
and
2.22
. It is valid on any conformal manifold of any dimension.

Proposition 4.1

(
[
34
,
35
]
)
.

The almost null structure of a pure conformal Killing spinor is locally integrable and co-integrable if and only if its associated tractor spinor is pure.

By Theorems
3.6
and
3.10
one can associate to any such conformal Killing spinor on
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
a complex submanifold in
\mathbb{PT}
. These are described in the next two propositions.

4.1.1
Odd dimensions

Proposition 4.2
.

Let
\Xi^{\bm{\upalpha}}=(\xi^{\mathbf{A}},\zeta^{\mathbf{A}})
be a constant pure tractor spinor on
\mathcal{Q}^{2m+1}
,
\Xi
its associated twistor in
\mathbb{PT}
,
\check{\Xi}
its corresponding
\gamma
-plane in
\mathcal{Q}^{2m+1}
, and
\mathcal{U}:=\mathcal{Q}^{2m+1}\setminus\check{\Xi}
. Then
\xi^{\mathbf{A}}
is a pure conformal Killing spinor on
\mathcal{Q}^{2m+1}
with zero set
\check{\Xi}
, and its associated integrable and co-integrable almost null structure
N_{\xi}
on
\mathcal{U}
arises from the submanifold
\mathbf{D}_{\Xi}\setminus\{\Xi\}
in
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}\subset\mathbb{PT}
, where
\mathbf{D}_{\Xi}
is given by (
2.17
). In particular, each leaf of
N_{\xi}
consists of a
\gamma
-plane intersecting
\check{\Xi}
in an
(m-1)
-plane. Each leaf of
N_{\xi}^{\perp}
consists of a
1
-parameter family of
\gamma
-planes intersecting in an
(m-1)
-plane. Any two
\gamma
-planes contained in two distinct leaves of
N_{\xi}^{\perp}
intersect in an
(m-2)
-plane.

Proof.

The line spanned by
\Xi^{\bm{\upalpha}}
descends to a point
\Xi
(i.e.,
[\Xi^{\bm{\upalpha}}]
) in
\mathbb{PT}
, and thus singles out a
\gamma
-plane
\check{\Xi}
in
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
, which by (
4.3
) can be immediately identified with the zero set of
\xi^{\mathbf{A}}
. Off that set, Proposition
4.1
tells us that
N_{\xi}
is integrable and co-integrable. Correspondingly, the conformal Killing spinor
\xi^{\mathbf{A}}
gives rise to a section
[\xi^{\mathbf{A}}]
of
\mathbb{F}
, which we can re-express as

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\xi}=\big\{([X^{\mathcal{A}}],[Z^{\bm{\upalpha}}])\in\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{PT}\colon Z^{{{\bm{\upalpha}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}=X^{\mathcal{A}}\Xi_{\mathcal{A}}^{\bm{\upalpha}}\big\}\subset\mathbb{F}.

Clearly, a point on
\Gamma_{\xi}
descends to a twistor
Z
on
\mathbf{D}_{\xi}\setminus\{\Xi\}
with
\gamma
-plane
\check{Z}
tangent to
N_{\xi}
. Thus, for each
Z
on
\mathbf{D}_{\Xi}\setminus\{\Xi\}
in
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}\subset\mathbb{PT}
,
\check{Z}
is precisely a leaf of
N_{\xi}
. The point
\Xi
itself must be excluded from
\mathbf{D}_{\Xi}
since the foliation becomes singular there in the sense the leaves intersect in
\check{\Xi}
. The geometric interpretation of the leaves of
N_{\xi}
and
N_{\xi}^{\perp}
follows directly from Theorem
2.11
and Corollary
2.14
. In particular, each distinguished curve on
\mathbf{D}_{\Xi}
can be identified with a leaf of
N_{\xi}^{\perp}
.
∎

Local form.
Let us re-express the
(m+1)
-plane
\mathbf{D}_{\Xi}
as (
2.18
). We work in a conformal scale for which
g_{ab}
is the flat metric. Since
\Xi^{\bm{\upalpha}}
is constant, we can substitute the fields for their constants of integration at the origin. Using (
2.30
) and
\Xi^{\bm{\upalpha}}=I^{\bm{\upalpha}}_{\mathbf{A}}\mathring{\xi}^{\mathbf{A}}+O^{\bm{\upalpha}}_{\mathbf{A}}\mathring{\zeta}^{\mathbf{A}}
, we obtain, in the obvious notation,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\omega^{{{a}{\mathbf{A}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\mathring{\xi}^{{{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{a}}}+2\mathring{\xi}^{\mathbf{A}}\omega^{\mathbf{B}}-\omega^{\mathbf{A}}\mathring{\xi}^{\mathbf{B}}=0,\\
&\pi^{{{a}{\mathbf{A}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\mathring{\zeta}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}+2\mathring{\zeta}^{\mathbf{A}}\pi^{\mathbf{B}}-\pi^{A}\mathring{\zeta}^{\mathbf{B}}=0,\\
&\omega^{{{a}{\mathbf{A}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\mathring{\zeta}^{{{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{a}}}+\omega^{\mathbf{A}}\mathring{\zeta}^{\mathbf{B}}+4\pi^{[{\mathbf{A}}}\mathring{\xi}^{{\mathbf{B}}]}=0,\\
&\pi^{{{a}{\mathbf{A}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\mathring{\xi}^{{{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{a}}}+\pi^{\mathbf{A}}\mathring{\xi}^{\mathbf{B}}+4\omega^{[{\mathbf{A}}}\mathring{\zeta}^{{\mathbf{B}}]}=0.\end{split}

(4.4)

Evaluating at
\omega^{\mathbf{A}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}x^{a}\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{A}}}}_{{{a}{\mathbf{B}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\pi^{\mathbf{B}}
, using the second and third of (
4.4
) together with the purity of
\Xi^{\bm{\upalpha}}
, we find that
\pi^{\mathbf{A}}
must be proportional to
\xi^{\mathbf{A}}=\mathring{\xi}^{\mathbf{A}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}x^{a}\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{A}}}}_{{{a}{\mathbf{B}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\mathring{\zeta}^{\mathbf{B}}
as expected. This solution then satisfies the first and fourth equations.

Let us now work in the coordinate chart
(\mathcal{V}_{0},(\omega^{0},\omega^{A},\pi^{A},\pi^{AB}))
as defined in Section
A.1
, and write

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathring{\xi}^{\mathbf{A}}=\mathring{\xi}^{0}o^{\mathbf{A}}+\mathrm{i}\frac{1}{2}\mathring{\xi}^{A}\delta_{A}^{\mathbf{A}}-\frac{1}{4}\mathring{\xi}^{AB}\delta_{AB}^{\mathbf{A}}+\cdots,\\
&\mathring{\zeta}^{\mathbf{A}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\mathrm{i}\mathring{\zeta}^{0}o^{\mathbf{A}}+\mathring{\zeta}^{A}\delta_{A}^{\mathbf{A}}-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{4\mathring{\xi}^{0}}\left(\mathring{\xi}^{AB}\mathring{\zeta}^{0}-2\mathring{\xi}^{A}\mathring{\zeta}^{B}\right)\delta_{AB}^{\mathbf{A}}+\cdots\right),\end{split}

(4.5)

where the remaining components of
\mathring{\zeta}^{\mathbf{A}}
and
\mathring{\xi}^{\mathbf{A}}
depend only on
\mathring{\zeta}^{0}
,
\mathring{\zeta}^{A}
,
\mathring{\xi}^{A}
and
\mathring{\xi}^{AB}
by the purity of
\Xi^{{\bm{\upalpha}}}
, and where we have assumed
\mathring{\xi}^{0}\neq 0
.
Substituting (
A.7
) and (
4.5
) into the last of equations (
4.4
) yields

\displaystyle\mathring{\xi}^{0}\pi^{A}-\mathring{\xi}^{A}+\mathring{\zeta}^{0}\omega^{A}-\omega^{0}\mathring{\zeta}^{A}=0,\qquad\mathring{\xi}^{0}\pi^{AB}-\mathring{\xi}^{AB}+2\omega^{[A}\mathring{\zeta}^{B]}=0,

while the remaining equations do not yield any new information. Now, at every point
Z
of
\mathbf{D}_{\Xi}
, the
1
-forms

\displaystyle\bm{\beta}^{A}:=\mathring{\xi}^{0}\mathrm{d}\pi^{A}+\mathring{\zeta}^{0}\mathrm{d}\omega^{A}-\mathring{\zeta}^{A}\mathrm{d}\omega^{0},\qquad\bm{\beta}^{AB}:=\mathring{\xi}^{0}\mathrm{d}\pi^{AB}+2\mathrm{d}\omega^{[A}\mathring{\zeta}^{B]},

annihilate the vectors tangent to
\mathbf{D}_{\Xi}
at
Z
and the line in
{\mathrm{D}}_{Z}
spanned by

\displaystyle\bm{V}:=V^{0}\bm{Y}+V^{A}\bm{Y}_{A},

(4.6)

where
V^{0}:=\mathring{\xi}^{0}+\frac{1}{2}\mathring{\zeta}^{0}\omega^{0}
and
V^{A}:=\mathring{\zeta}^{A}+\frac{1}{2}\mathring{\zeta}^{0}\pi^{A}
. This corroborates the claims of Theorem
3.6
and Proposition
4.2
. Note that the vector field
\bm{V}
vanishes at the point
[\Xi^{\mathbf{\upalpha}}]
of
\mathbf{D}_{\Xi}
. With no loss, we can set
\mathring{\zeta}^{0}=-2
. The integral curve, with complex parameter
t
, of (
4.6
) passing through the point

\displaystyle\big(\omega^{0},\omega^{A},\pi^{A},\pi^{AB}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\left(\mathring{\xi}^{0}+\alpha,-\frac{1}{2}\big(\mathring{\xi}^{A}+\alpha\mathring{\zeta}^{A}-\mathring{\xi}^{0}\alpha^{A}\big),\mathring{\zeta}^{A}+\alpha^{A},\frac{1}{\mathring{\xi}^{0}}\big(\mathring{\xi}^{AB}+\mathring{\xi}^{[A}\mathring{\zeta}^{B]}\big)-\alpha^{[A}\mathring{\zeta}^{B]}\right),

for some
\alpha
,
\alpha^{A}
, is given by

\displaystyle\big(\omega^{0}(t),\omega^{A}(t),\pi^{A}(t),\pi^{AB}(t)\big)=\left(\mathring{\xi}^{0},-\frac{1}{2}\mathring{\xi}^{A},\mathring{\zeta}^{A},\frac{1}{\mathring{\xi}^{0}}\big(\mathring{\xi}^{AB}+\mathring{\xi}^{[A}\mathring{\zeta}^{B]}\big)\right)

\displaystyle\hphantom{\big( \omega^0 (t), \omega^A (t), \pi^A (t), \pi^{AB} (t)\big) =}{}+\left(\alpha,-\frac{1}{2}\big(\alpha\mathring{\zeta}^{A}-\mathring{\xi}^{0}\alpha^{A}\big),\alpha^{A},-\alpha^{[A}\mathring{\zeta}^{B]}\right)\mathrm{e}^{-t},

Writing
A^{\mathcal{A}}=aY^{\mathcal{A}}+A^{a}Z_{a}^{\mathcal{A}}+bX^{\mathcal{A}}
and
A^{a}=A^{A}\delta_{A}^{a}+A_{A}\delta^{aA}+A^{0}u^{a}
with

\displaystyle\alpha=-a-\frac{1}{2}A^{0}\mathring{\xi}^{0}+\frac{1}{2}A_{C}\mathring{\xi}^{C},

\displaystyle\alpha^{A}=\frac{1}{2\mathring{\xi}^{0}}\big(A_{C}\mathring{\xi}^{C}\mathring{\zeta}^{A}-A^{0}\mathring{\xi}^{0}\mathring{\zeta}^{A}-2\mathring{\xi}^{AB}A_{B}-A^{0}\mathring{\xi}^{A}-2\mathring{\xi}^{0}A^{A}\big),

\displaystyle b=\frac{1}{\mathring{\xi}^{0}}\big(A^{0}-\mathring{\zeta}^{C}A_{C}\big),

one can recast this integral curve tractorially as
Z^{\bm{\upalpha}}(t)=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\sqrt{2}}\big(\mathring{\Xi}^{\bm{\upalpha}}+\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\mathrm{e}^{-t}\mathring{A}^{\mathcal{A}}\mathring{\Xi}^{\bm{\upalpha}}_{\mathcal{A}}\big)
, which is one of the distinguished curves of Lemma
2.10
as expected.

4.1.2
Even dimensions

In even dimensions, the story is entirely analogous except for the choice of chirality of the tractor spinor. We leave the details to the reader.

Proposition 4.3
.

Let
\Xi^{\bm{\upalpha^{\prime}}}=(\xi^{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}},\zeta^{\mathbf{A}})
be a constant pure tractor spinor on
\mathcal{Q}^{2m}
, and let
\mathcal{U}:=\mathcal{Q}^{2m}\setminus\check{\Xi}
where
\check{\Xi}
is the
\beta
-plane defined by
\Xi^{\bm{\alpha^{\prime}}}
. Then
\xi^{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}}
is a pure conformal Killing spinor on
\mathcal{Q}^{2m}
, and its associated null structure
N_{\xi}
on
\mathcal{U}
arises from the submanifold in
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}\subset\mathbb{PT}
defined by

\displaystyle\Gamma^{{{(k)}}}_{{{\bm{\upalpha}}{{\bm{\upbeta^{\prime}}}}}}Z^{{{\bm{\upalpha}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\Xi^{{{{\bm{\upbeta^{\prime}}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3819pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.3819pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.23405pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.44218pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}=0,\qquad\text{for}\quad k<m,\quad k\equiv m\pmod{2}.

(4.7)

Each leaf of
N_{\xi}
consists of an
\alpha
-plane intersecting
\check{\Xi}
in an
(m-1)
-plane.

Remark 4.4
.

In four dimensions, tractor-spinors are always pure, and so almost null structures associated to conformal Killing spinors are always integrable. In this case, the submanifold (
4.7
) is a complex projective hyperplane in
\mathbb{PT}\cong\mathbb{CP}^{3}
given by
\Xi_{\bm{\upalpha}}Z^{\bm{\upalpha}}=0
where we have used the canonical isomorphism
\mathbb{PT}^{*}\cong\mathbb{PT}^{\prime}
. This example was highly instrumental in the genesis of twistor theory
[
29
]
. The null structure arising from the intersection of this submanifold with
real
twistor space generates a shearfree congruence of null geodesics in Minkowski space known as the
Robinson congruence
.

4.2
Conformal Killing–Yano 2-forms

A (holomorphic)
conformal Killing–Yano
(
CKY
)

2
-form
on
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
is a section
\sigma_{ab}
of
\mathcal{O}_{[ab]}[3]
that satisfies

\displaystyle\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a}}}\sigma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.09041pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.09041pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.2234pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.01671pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{bc}}}-\mu^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.81349pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.86676pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{abc}}}-2\mathbf{g}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.11821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.11821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.05411pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.32434pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a[b}}}\varphi^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.83765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.83765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.48161pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.48685pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{c]}}}=0,

(4.8)

where
\mu_{abc}=\nabla_{[a}\sigma_{bc]}
and
\varphi_{a}=\frac{1}{n-2}\nabla^{b}\sigma_{ba}
.
The CKY
2
-form equation (
4.8
) is prolonged to the following system

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a}}}\sigma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.09041pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.09041pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.2234pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.01671pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{bc}}}-\mu^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.81349pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.86676pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{abc}}}-2\mathbf{g}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.11821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.11821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.05411pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.32434pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a[b}}}\varphi^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.83765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.83765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.48161pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.48685pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{c]}}}=0,\\
&\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a}}}\mu^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.25328pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.25328pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.77379pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.83841pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{bcd}}}+3\mathbf{g}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.11821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.11821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.05411pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.32434pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a[b}}}\rho^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.00052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.00052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.032pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.30855pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{cd]}}}+3\mathrm{P}_{a[b}\sigma_{cd]}=0,\\
&\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a}}}\varphi_{b}-\rho_{ab}+\mathrm{P}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\sigma_{cb}=0,\\
&\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a}}}\rho_{bc}-\mathrm{P}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{d}}}_{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\mu_{dbc}+2\mathrm{P}_{a[b}\varphi_{c]}=0.\end{split}

(4.9)

This system can be seen to be equivalent to the existence of a parallel tractor
3
-form, i.e.,

\displaystyle\nabla_{a}\Sigma_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}\mathcal{C}}=0,

(4.10)

where
\Sigma_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}\mathcal{C}}:=(\sigma_{ab},\mu_{abc},\varphi_{a},\rho_{ab})\in\mathcal{O}_{[\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}\mathcal{C}]}\cong\mathcal{O}_{[ab]}[3]+(\mathcal{O}_{[abc]}[3]\oplus\mathcal{O}_{a}[1])+\mathcal{O}_{[ab]}[1]
. For an arbitrary conformal manifold, equation (
4.10
) no longer holds in general, and necessitates the addition of a ‘deformation’ term as explained in
[
15
]
.

In flat space, i.e., with
\mathrm{P}_{ab}=0
, we can integrate equations (
4.9
) to obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sigma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.693pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.35213pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ab}}}=\mathring{\sigma}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.693pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.35213pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ab}}}+2x_{[a}\mathring{\varphi}_{b]}+\mathring{\mu}_{abc}x^{c}-2\big(x_{[a}\mathring{\rho}_{b]c}x^{c}+\tfrac{1}{4}\big(x^{c}x_{c}\big)\mathring{\rho}_{ab}\big),\\
&\mu^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.81349pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.86676pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{abc}}}=\mathring{\mu}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.81349pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.86676pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{abc}}}-3x^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.60155pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.60155pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.9512pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82227pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{[a}}}\mathring{\rho}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.35431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.35431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.58452pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{bc]}}},\\
&\varphi^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a}}}=\mathring{\varphi}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a}}}-\mathring{\rho}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.693pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.35213pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ab}}}x^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}},\\
&\rho^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.693pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.35213pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ab}}}=\mathring{\rho}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.693pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.35213pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ab}}},\end{split}

(4.11)

for some constants
\mathring{\sigma}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.693pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.35213pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ab}}}
,
\mathring{\mu}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.81349pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.86676pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{abc}}}
,
\mathring{\varphi}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a}}}
and
\mathring{\rho}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.693pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.35213pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ab}}}
.

Remark 4.5
.

In three dimensions, conformal Killing–Yano
2
-forms are Hodge dual to conformal Killing vector fields. These latter are in one-to-one correspondence with parallel sections of tractor
2
-forms.

In four dimensions, a
2
-form
\sigma_{ab}
is a CKY
2
-form if and only if its self-dual part
\sigma^{+}_{ab}
and its anti-self-dual part
\sigma^{-}_{ab}
are CKY
2
-forms, with, in the obvious notation,
\mu^{{{\pm}}}_{{{abc}}}=(*\varphi^{\pm})^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.81349pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.86676pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{abc}}}
. Self-duality obviously carries over to tractor
3
-forms.

4.2.1
Eigenspinors of a 2-form

Let us first assume
n=2m+1
. We recall that an
eigenspinor

\xi^{\mathbf{A}}
of a
2
-form
\sigma_{ab}
is a spinor satisfying

\displaystyle\sigma_{ab}\gamma^{{{ab}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.06969pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.90692pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{[{\mathbf{A}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.693pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.35213pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{{\mathbf{C}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.0389pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.0389pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.62137pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01524pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\xi^{{\mathbf{B}}]}\xi^{{\mathbf{C}}}=0,

(4.12)

i.e.,
\sigma_{ab}\gamma^{{{ab}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.06969pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.90692pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{A}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.693pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.35213pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{C}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\xi^{\mathbf{C}}=\lambda\xi^{\mathbf{A}}
for some function
\lambda
. Here,
\gamma^{{{ab}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.06969pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.90692pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{A}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.693pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.35213pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{C}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}:=\gamma^{{{[a}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.06969pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.90692pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.60155pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.60155pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.9512pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82227pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{C}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\gamma^{{{b]}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{A}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.78056pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.78056pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.46402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.47429pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}
. When
\xi^{\mathbf{A}}
is pure, another convenient way to express the eigenspinor equation (
4.12
) is given by

\displaystyle\sigma^{ab}\gamma^{{{(m+1)}}}_{{{abc_{3}\ldots c_{m+1}}{{\mathbf{AB}}}}}\xi^{\mathbf{A}}\xi^{\mathbf{B}}=0.

Therefore, to any
2
-form
\sigma_{ab}
, we can associate a complex submanifold of
\mathbb{F}
given by the graph

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\sigma}:=\big\{\big(x^{a},\big[\pi^{\mathbf{A}}\big]\big)\in\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}\times\mathbb{PT}_{(2m-1)}\colon\sigma^{ab}\gamma^{{{(m+1)}}}_{{{abc_{3}\ldots c_{m+1}}{{\mathbf{AB}}}}}\pi^{\mathbf{A}}\pi^{\mathbf{B}}=0\big\}.

(4.13)

For
\sigma_{ab}
generic, this submanifold will have many connected components, each of which corresponding to a local section of
\mathbb{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{Q}^{2m+1}
, i.e., a projective pure spinor field that is an eigenspinor of
\sigma_{ab}
. To be precise, in
2m+1
dimensions, a generic
2
-form
\sigma_{ab}
viewed as an endomorphism
\sigma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}_{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}
of the tangent bundle, always has
m
distinct pairs of non-zero eigenvalues opposite to each other, i.e.,
(\lambda,-\lambda)
, and a zero eigenvalue. In this case, a generic
2
-form viewed as an element of the Clifford algebra has
2^{m}
distinct eigenvalues, and thus
2^{m}
distinct eigenspaces, all of whose elements are pure
[
26
]
.

When
n=2m
, the analysis is very similar: the pure eigenspinor equation is now

\displaystyle\sigma^{ab}\gamma^{{{(m)}}}_{{{abc_{3}\ldots c_{m}}{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}\mathbf{B^{\prime}}}}}\xi^{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}}\xi^{\mathbf{B^{\prime}}}=0,

and similarity for spinors of the opposite chirality. Such a
2
-form generically has
m
distinct pairs of non-zero eigenvalues opposite to each other, and as an element of the Clifford algebra, has
2^{m}
eigenspaces that split into two sets of
2^{m-1}
eigenspaces according to the chirality of the eigenspinors. The eigenspinor equation lifts to a submanifold
\Gamma_{\sigma}:=\{(x^{a},[\pi^{{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}}}])\in\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}\times\mathbb{PT}_{(2m-2)}\colon\sigma^{ab}\gamma^{{{(m)}}}_{{{abc_{3}\ldots c_{m}}{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}\mathbf{B^{\prime}}}}}\pi^{{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}}}\pi^{{\mathbf{B^{\prime}}}}=0\}
of
\mathbb{F}
, whose connected components correspond to the distinct primed spinor eigenspaces of
\sigma_{ab}
.

4.2.2
The null structures of a conformal Killing–Yano 2-forms

The next question to address is when the almost null structure of an eigenspinor of a
2
-form is integrable and co-integrable.

Proposition 4.6

(
[
26
]
)
.

Let
\sigma_{ab}
be a generic conformal Killing
2
-form on
\mathcal{Q}^{n}

(
or any complex Riemannian manifold
)
. Let
\mu_{abc}:=\nabla_{[a}\sigma_{bc]}
. Let
N
be the almost null structure of some eigenspinor of
\sigma_{ab}
, and suppose that
\mu_{abc}X^{a}Y^{b}Z^{c}=0
for any sections
X^{a}
,
Y^{a}
,
Z^{a}
of
N^{\perp}
. Then
N
is integrable and, when
n
is odd, co-integrable too.

In the light of Theorems
3.6
and
3.10
, the foliations arising from the eigenspinors of a CKY
2
-form
\sigma_{ab}
can be encoded as complex submanifolds of the twistor space
\mathbb{PT}
of
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
. As we shall see in a moment, these submanifolds can be constructed from the corresponding tractor
\Sigma_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}\mathcal{C}}
.

The additional condition on
\mu_{abc}
in Proposition
4.6
can also be understood in terms of the graph of a connected component of
\Gamma_{\sigma}
defined by (
4.13
). For such a graph to descend to a complex submanifold of
\mathbb{PT}
, its defining equations should be annihilated by the vectors tangent to
\mathbb{F}\rightarrow\mathbb{PT}
. Such a condition, in odd dimensions, can be expressed as
0=\pi^{[\mathbf{C}}\pi^{{{c}{\mathbf{D}]}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.15971pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.15971pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.68262pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.05899pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\nabla_{c}(\sigma_{ab}\pi^{a\mathbf{A}}\pi^{b\mathbf{B}})
, and using (
4.8
) gives
\mu_{abc}\pi^{a\mathbf{A}}\pi^{b\mathbf{B}}\pi^{b\mathbf{C}}=0
. Thus, we shall be interested in the local sections of
\mathbb{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{Q}^{n}
defined by

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\sigma,\mu}:=\big\{\big(x^{a},\big[\pi^{\mathbf{A}}\big]\big)\in\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}\times\mathbb{PT}_{(2m-1)}\colon

\displaystyle\hphantom{\Gamma_{\sigma,\mu} := \big\lx@text@lbrace}{}\sigma^{ab}\gamma^{{{(m+1)}}}_{{{abc_{3}\ldots c_{m+1}}{{\mathbf{AB}}}}}\pi^{\mathbf{A}}\pi^{\mathbf{B}}=0,\,\mu^{abc}\gamma^{{{(m+1)}}}_{{{abcd_{4}\ldots d_{m+1}}{\mathbf{AB}}}}\pi^{\mathbf{A}}\pi^{\mathbf{B}}=0\big\}.

(4.14)

In even dimensions, this is entirely analogous except that (
4.14
) is now

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\sigma,\mu}:=\big\{\big(x^{a},\big[\pi^{{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}}}\big]\big)\in\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}\times\mathbb{PT}_{(2m-2)}\colon

\displaystyle\hphantom{\Gamma_{\sigma,\mu} := \big\lx@text@lbrace}{}\sigma^{ab}\gamma^{{{(m)}}}_{{{abc_{3}\ldots c_{m}}{{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}}{\mathbf{B^{\prime}}}}}}\pi^{{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}}}\pi^{{\mathbf{B^{\prime}}}}=0,\,\mu^{{{abc}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.81349pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.86676pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\gamma^{{{(m)}}}_{{{abcd_{4}\ldots d_{m}}{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}\mathbf{B^{\prime}}}}}\pi^{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}}\pi^{\mathbf{B^{\prime}}}=0\big\}.

Proposition 4.7
.

Set
n=2m+\epsilon
, where
\epsilon\in\{0,1\}
. Let
\sigma_{ab}
be a generic conformal Killing–Yano
2
-form on some open subset
\mathcal{U}
of
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
, with associated tractor
3
-form
\Sigma_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}\mathcal{C}}
. Then if the almost null structure associated to some eigenspinor of
\sigma_{ab}
is integrable and co-integrable, it must arise from the submanifold in
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}\subset\mathbb{PT}
defined by

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}\mathcal{C}}\Gamma^{{{(m+1+\epsilon)}}}_{{{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}\mathcal{C}\mathcal{D}_{4}\ldots\mathcal{D}_{m+1+\epsilon}}{{\bm{\upalpha}}{\bm{\upbeta}}}}}Z^{\bm{\upalpha}}Z^{\bm{\upbeta}}=0.

(4.15)

Proof.

We focus on the odd-dimensional case only, and leave the even-dimensional case to the reader. Let us write

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}\mathcal{C}}=3Y_{[\mathcal{A}}Z_{\mathcal{B}}^{b}Z_{\mathcal{C}]}^{c}\sigma_{bc}+\big(Z_{\mathcal{A}}^{a}Z_{\mathcal{B}}^{b}Z_{\mathcal{C}}^{c}\mu_{abc}+6X_{[\mathcal{A}}Y_{\mathcal{B}}Z_{\mathcal{C}]}^{c}\varphi_{c}\big)+3X_{[\mathcal{A}}Z_{\mathcal{B}}^{b}Z_{\mathcal{C}]}^{c}\rho_{bc}.

Since
\Sigma_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}\mathcal{C}}
is constant, we can substitute the fields for their constants of integration at the origin,
\mathring{\sigma}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.693pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.35213pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ab}}}
,
\mathring{\mu}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.81349pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.86676pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{abc}}}
,
\mathring{\varphi}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a}}}
and
\mathring{\rho}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.693pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.35213pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ab}}}
, so that using (
2.30
) we can re-express (
4.15
) as

\displaystyle 0=-3\sqrt{2}\mathring{\sigma}^{ab}\gamma^{{{(m+1)}}}_{{{abd_{4}\ldots d_{m+2}}{\mathbf{AB}}}}\pi^{\mathbf{A}}\pi^{\mathbf{B}}+2\mathring{\mu}^{abc}\gamma^{{{(m+2)}}}_{{{abcd_{4}\ldots d_{m+2}\mathbf{AB}}}}\omega^{\mathbf{A}}\pi^{\mathbf{B}}-12\mathring{\varphi}^{a}\gamma^{{{(m)}}}_{{{ad_{4}\ldots d_{m+2}\mathbf{AB}}}}\omega^{\mathbf{A}}\pi^{\mathbf{B}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{0=}{}+3\sqrt{2}\mathring{\rho}^{ab}\gamma^{{{(m+1)}}}_{{{abd_{4}\ldots d_{m+2}\mathbf{AB}}}}\omega^{\mathbf{A}}\omega^{\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle 0=\sqrt{2}\mathring{\mu}^{abc}\gamma^{{{(m+1)}}}_{{{abcd_{4}\ldots d_{m+1}}{\mathbf{AB}}}}\pi^{\mathbf{A}}\pi^{\mathbf{B}}-6\mathring{\rho}^{ab}\gamma^{{{(m)}}}_{{{abd_{4}\ldots d_{m+1}\mathbf{AB}}}}\omega^{\mathbf{A}}\pi^{\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle 0=-\sqrt{2}\mathring{\mu}^{abc}\gamma^{{{(m+1)}}}_{{{abcd_{4}\ldots d_{m+1}}{\mathbf{AB}}}}\omega^{\mathbf{A}}\omega^{\mathbf{B}}+6\mathring{\sigma}^{ab}\gamma^{{{(m)}}}_{{{abd_{4}\ldots d_{m+1}\mathbf{AB}}}}\omega^{\mathbf{A}}\pi^{\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle 0=2\mathring{\mu}^{abc}\gamma^{{{(m)}}}_{{{abcd_{4}\ldots d_{m}}{\mathbf{AB}}}}\omega^{\mathbf{A}}\pi^{\mathbf{B}}.

Evaluating this system of equations on the intersection of (
4.15
) and
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}
amounts to setting
\omega^{\mathbf{A}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}x^{a}\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{A}}}}_{{{a}{\mathbf{B}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\pi^{\mathbf{B}}
, and we find, after some algebraic manipulations,

\displaystyle 0=-3\sqrt{2}\big(\sigma^{ab}\gamma^{{{(m+1)}}}_{{{abd_{4}\ldots d_{m+2}}{\mathbf{AB}}}}\pi^{\mathbf{A}}\pi^{\mathbf{B}}\big)+\sqrt{2}(m-1)\big(x_{[d_{4}|}\mu^{abc}\gamma^{{{(m+1)}}}_{{{abc|d_{5}\ldots d_{m+2}]\mathbf{AB}}}}\pi^{\mathbf{A}}\pi^{\mathbf{B}}\big),

\displaystyle 0=\sqrt{2}\mu^{abc}\gamma^{{{(m+1)}}}_{{{abcd_{4}\ldots d_{m+1}}{\mathbf{AB}}}}\pi^{\mathbf{A}}\pi^{\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle 0=-\frac{(x^{e}x_{e})}{\sqrt{2}}\mu^{abc}\gamma^{{{(m+1)}}}_{{{abcd_{4}\ldots d_{m+1}}{\mathbf{AB}}}}\pi^{\mathbf{A}}\pi^{\mathbf{B}}+3\sqrt{2}\sigma^{ab}x^{c}\gamma^{{{(m+1)}}}_{{{abcd_{4}\ldots d_{m+1}\mathbf{AB}}}}\pi^{\mathbf{B}}\pi^{\mathbf{B}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{0=}{}+\sqrt{2}(m-2)x_{[d_{4}|}\mu^{abc}x^{f}\gamma^{{{(m+1)}}}_{{{abcf|d_{5}\ldots d_{m+1}]}{\mathbf{AB}}}}\pi^{\mathbf{B}}\pi^{\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle 0=\sqrt{2}\mu^{abc}x^{d}\gamma^{{{(m+1)}}}_{{{abcde_{5}\ldots e_{m+1}}{\mathbf{AB}}}}\pi^{\mathbf{A}}\pi^{\mathbf{B}},

where we have made use of (
4.11
) and the identity
