\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{4}\big(x^{c}\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.06969pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.90692pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{A}}}}_{{{c}{\mathbf{C}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\big)\big(\mathring{\rho}_{ab}\gamma^{{{ab}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.693pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.35213pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{A}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\big)\big(x^{d}\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{D}}}}_{{{d}{\mathbf{B}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.89581pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.89581pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.3215pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.08678pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\big)=\big(x_{a}\mathring{\rho}_{bc}x^{c}+\tfrac{1}{4}(x^{c}x_{c})\mathring{\rho}_{ab}\big)\gamma^{{{ab}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.06969pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.90692pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{D}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.693pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.35213pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{C}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.89581pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.89581pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.3215pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.08678pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}.

In particular, we immediately recover, that on the intersection of the twistor submanifold (
4.15
) with
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}
,

\displaystyle\sigma^{ab}\gamma^{{{(m+1)}}}_{{{abc_{3}\ldots c_{m+1}}{\mathbf{AB}}}}\pi^{\mathbf{A}}\pi^{\mathbf{B}}=0,\qquad\mu^{{{abc}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.81349pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.86676pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\gamma^{{{(m+1)}}}_{{{abcd_{4}\ldots d_{m+1}}{\mathbf{AB}}}}\pi^{\mathbf{A}}\pi^{\mathbf{B}}=0.

But these are precisely the zero set (
4.14
) corresponding to the eigenspinors of
\sigma_{ab}
.
∎

Remark 4.8
.

In three dimensions, the twistor submanifold is simply a smooth quadric in
\mathbb{PT}\cong\mathbb{CP}^{3}
.

In four dimensions, the submanifold (
4.15
) restricts to an anti-self-dual tractor
3
-form
\Sigma^{-}_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}\mathcal{C}}
corresponding to a self-dual CKY
2
-form
\sigma_{ab}
. Setting
\Sigma^{-}_{{\bm{\upalpha}}{\bm{\upbeta}}}:=\Sigma^{-}_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}\mathcal{C}}\Gamma^{{{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}\mathcal{C}}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[17.20148pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[17.20148pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.68474pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.63194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{{\bm{\upalpha}}{\bm{\upbeta}}}}}
, we recover the quadratic polynomial
\Sigma^{-}_{{\bm{\upalpha}}{\bm{\upbeta}}}Z^{{{\bm{\upalpha}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}Z^{{{\bm{\upbeta}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}=0
given in
[
31
]
. Under appropriate reality conditions, this submanifold produces a shearfree congruence of null geodesics in Minkowski space known as the
Kerr congruence
. A suitable perturbation of Minkowski space by the generator of such a congruence leads to the solution of Einstein’s equations known as the
Kerr metric

[
22
,
23
]
. A Euclidean analogue is also given in
[
32
]
.

In six dimensions, we have a splitting of
\mu_{abc}=\mu^{+}_{abc}+\mu^{-}_{abc}
into a self-dual part and an anti-self-dual part. Since
\xi^{a\mathbf{A}}\xi^{b\mathbf{B}}\xi^{c\mathbf{C}}\mathring{\mu}^{+}_{abc}=0
for any
\xi^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}
, the obstruction to the integrability of a positive eigenspinor of a generic CKY
2
-form
\sigma_{ab}
is the anti-self-dual part
\mu^{-}_{abc}
of
\mu_{abc}
.

5
Curved spaces

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a complex manifold equipped with a holomorphic non-degenerate symmetric bilinear form
g_{ab}
. The pair
(\mathcal{M},g_{ab})
will be referred to as a
complex Riemannian manifold
. We assume that
\mathcal{M}
is equipped with a holomorphic complex orientation and a holomorphic spin structure. We may also assume that one merely has a holomorphic conformal structure rather than a metric one. For definiteness, we set
n=2m+1
as the dimension of
\mathcal{M}
. The analogue of the correspondence space
\mathbb{F}
is the projective pure spinor bundle
\nu\colon\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}
: for any
x\in\mathcal{M}
, a point
p
in a fiber
\nu^{-1}(x)
is a totally null
m
-plane in
\mathrm{T}_{x}\mathcal{M}
, and sections of
\mathcal{F}
are almost null structures on
\mathcal{M}
. To define the twistor space of
(\mathcal{M},g_{ab})
, one must replace the notion of
\gamma
-plane by that of
\gamma
-surface
, i.e., an
m
-dimensional complex submanifold of
\mathcal{M}
such that at any point of such a surface, its tangent space is totally null with respect to the metric and totally geodetic with respect to the metric connection. The integrability condition for the existence of a
\gamma
-surface
\mathcal{N}
through a point
x
is
[
35
]

\displaystyle C_{abcd}X^{a}Y^{b}Z^{c}W^{d}=0,\qquad\text{for all}\quad X^{a},Y^{a},Z^{c}\in\mathrm{T}_{x}\mathcal{N},\quad W^{a}\in\mathrm{T}_{x}\mathcal{N}.

(5.1)

If we define the twistor space of
(\mathcal{M},g_{ab})
to be the
\frac{1}{2}(m+1)(m+2)
-dimensional complex manifold parametrising the
\gamma
-surfaces of
(\mathcal{M},g_{ab})
, we must have a
\frac{1}{2}m(m+1)
-parameter family of
\gamma
-surfaces through each point of
\mathcal{M}
. From the integrability condition (
5.1
), we must conclude that for the twistor space of
(\mathcal{M},g_{ab})
to exist,
(\mathcal{M},g_{ab})
must be conformally flat in odd dimensions greater than three. In even dimensions the story is similar: one replaces the notion of
\alpha
-plane by that of an
\alpha
-surface in the obvious way. We then find that for
(\mathcal{M},g_{ab})
to admit a twistor space, it must be conformally flat in even dimensions greater than four, and anti-self-dual in dimension four.

Curved twistor theory in dimensions three and four is pretty well-known. In dimension four, we have the
Penrose correspondence
, whereby twistor space is a three-dimensional complex manifold containing a complete analytic family of rational curves with normal bundle
\mathcal{O}(1)\oplus\mathcal{O}(1)
parameterised by the points of an anti-self-dual complex Riemannian manifold
[
30
]
. In dimension three, the
LeBrun correspondence
can be seen as a special case of the Penrose correspondence: if we endow twistor space with a holomorphic ‘twisted’ contact structure, then a three-dimensional conformal manifold arises as the umbilic conformal infinity of an Einstein anti-self-dual four-dimensional complex Riemannian manifold
[
25
]
. Finally, the mini-twistor space in the
Hitchin correspondence
is a two-dimensional complex manifold containing a complete analytic family of rational curves with normal bundle
\mathcal{O}(2)
parameterised by the points of an Einstein–Weyl space
[
18
,
21
]
.

Theorems
3.5
(or
3.6
),
3.8
and
3.10
can be adapted to the curved setting by interpreting the leaf space of a totally geodetic null foliation as a complex submanifold of twistor space. See
[
8
]
for an application of a ‘curved’ Theorem
3.8
in the investigation of three-dimensional Einstein–Weyl spaces.

Appendix A
Coordinate charts on twistor space and correspondence space

In this appendix, we construct atlases of coordinates charts covering
\mathbb{PT}
and
\mathbb{F}
. We refer to the setup of Section
2
throughout. In particular, we work with the splittings (
2.3
), (
2.8
) and (
2.12
).

A.1
Odd dimensions

Let us introduce a splitting of
\mathbb{V}_{0}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}_{0}\cong\mathbb{W}\oplus\mathbb{W}^{*}\oplus\mathbb{U},

(A.1)

where
\mathbb{W}\cong\mathbb{C}^{m}
is a totally null
m
-plane of
(\mathbb{V}_{0},g_{ab})
, and
\mathbb{U}\cong\mathbb{C}
is the one-dimensional complement of
\mathbb{W}\oplus\mathbb{W}^{*}
in
\mathbb{V}_{0}
. Elements of
\mathbb{W}
and
\mathbb{W}^{*}
will carry upstairs and downstairs upper-case Roman indices respectively, i.e.,
V^{A}\in\mathbb{W}
, and
W_{A}\in\mathbb{W}^{*}
. The vector subspace
\mathbb{U}
will be spanned by a unit vector
u^{a}
. Denote by
\delta^{aA}
the injector from
\mathbb{W}^{*}
to
\mathbb{V}_{0}
, and
\delta^{a}_{A}
the injector from
\mathbb{W}
to
\mathbb{V}_{0}
satisfying
\delta_{a}^{A}\delta^{a}_{B}=\delta_{B}^{A}
, where
\delta_{B}^{A}
is the identity on
\mathbb{W}
and
\mathbb{W}^{*}
. We shall think of
\{\delta^{aA}\}
as a basis for
\mathbb{W}
with dual basis
\{\delta^{a}_{A}\}
for
\mathbb{W}^{*}
. The splitting (
A.1
) allows us to identify the two copies
\mathbb{S}_{\pm\frac{1}{2}}
of the spinor space of
(\mathbb{V}_{0},g_{ab})
with its Fock representation, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}_{\pm\frac{1}{2}}\cong\wedge^{m}\mathbb{W}\oplus\wedge^{m-1}\mathbb{W}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathbb{W}\oplus\mathbb{C}.

This is essentially the strategy adopted in Section
2.2
for the spinors of
\mathrm{Spin}(2m+3,\mathbb{C})
. To realise it explicitly, we proceed as follows: let
o^{\mathbf{A}}
be a (pure) spinor annihilating
\mathbb{W}
so that
o^{\mathbf{A}}
is a spanning element of
\wedge^{m}\mathbb{W}
. A (Fock) basis for
\mathbb{S}_{\pm\frac{1}{2}}
can then be produced by acting on
o^{\mathbf{A}}
by basis elements of
\wedge^{\bullet}\mathbb{W}^{*}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}_{\pm\frac{1}{2}}=\big\langle o^{\mathbf{A}},\delta^{\mathbf{A}}_{A_{1}},\delta^{\mathbf{A}}_{A_{1}A_{2}},\ldots\big\rangle,

(A.2)

where

\displaystyle\delta^{\mathbf{A}}_{A_{1}\ldots A_{k}}:=\delta_{[A_{1}}^{a_{1}}\cdots\delta_{A_{k}]}^{a_{k}}o^{\mathbf{A_{0}}}\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.09247pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.09247pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.77274pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55551pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{A_{1}}}}}_{{{a_{1}}{\mathbf{A_{0}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.09247pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.09247pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.77274pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55551pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\cdots\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.02237pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.28233pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.6998pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.6998pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.06367pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.84645pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{A}}}}_{{{a_{k}}{\mathbf{A_{k-1}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}},

for each
k=1,\ldots,m
. With this notation, the Clifford multiplication of
\mathbb{V}_{0}\subset{\mathcal{C}\ell}(\mathbb{V}_{0},g_{ab})
on
\mathbb{S}_{-\frac{1}{2}}
is given explicitly by

\displaystyle\delta^{aA}\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{C}}}}_{{{a}{\mathbf{B}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.06969pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.90692pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\delta^{{{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{B_{1}\ldots B_{p}}}}=-2p\delta^{{{\mathbf{C}}}}_{{{[B_{1}\ldots B_{p-1}}}}\delta^{{{A}}}_{{{{B_{p}}]}}},\qquad

\displaystyle\delta^{a}_{A}\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{C}}}}_{{{a}{\mathbf{B}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.06969pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.90692pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\delta^{{{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{B_{1}\ldots B_{p}}}}=\delta^{{{\mathbf{C}}}}_{{{B_{1}\ldots B_{p}A}}},

\displaystyle u^{a}\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{C}}}}_{{{a}{\mathbf{B}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.06969pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.90692pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}o^{\mathbf{B}}=\mathrm{i}o^{\mathbf{C}},\qquad

\displaystyle u^{a}\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{C}}}}_{{{a}{\mathbf{B}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.51944pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.06969pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.90692pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\delta^{{{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{{B_{1}\ldots B_{p}}}}=(-1)^{p}\mathrm{i}\delta^{{{\mathbf{C}}}}_{{{B_{1}\ldots B_{p}}}}.

(A.3)

An arbitrary spinor
\pi^{\mathbf{A}}
in
\mathbb{S}_{\frac{1}{2}}
can then be expressed in the Fock basis (
A.2
) as

\displaystyle\pi^{\mathbf{A}}=\pi^{0}o^{\mathbf{A}}+\sum_{k=1}^{[m/2]}\left(-\frac{1}{4}\right)^{k}\frac{1}{k!}\pi^{A_{1}\ldots A_{2k}}\delta_{A_{1}\ldots A_{2k}}^{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\pi^{\mathbf{A}} =}{}+\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\sum_{k=0}^{[m/2]}\left(-\frac{1}{4}\right)^{k}\frac{1}{k!}\pi^{A_{1}\ldots A_{2k+1}}\delta_{A_{1}\ldots A_{2k+1}}^{\mathbf{A}},\qquad m>1,

\displaystyle\pi^{\mathbf{A}}=\pi^{0}o^{\mathbf{A}}+\tfrac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\pi^{A}\delta_{A}^{\mathbf{A}},\qquad m=1,

(A.4)

where
\left[\frac{m}{2}\right]
is
\frac{m}{2}
when
m
is even,
\frac{m-1}{2}
when
m
is odd, and
\pi^{0}
and
\pi^{A_{1}A_{2}\ldots A_{k}}=\pi^{[A_{1}A_{2}\ldots A_{k}]}
are the components of
\pi^{\mathbf{A}}
. Let us now assume that
\pi^{\mathbf{A}}
is pure, i.e., satisfies (
2.31a
). When
m=1
and
2
, there are no algebraic constraints, and the space of projective pure spinors is isomorphic to
\mathbb{CP}^{1}
and
\mathbb{CP}^{3}
respectively. When
m>2
, the pure spinor variety is then given by the complete intersections of the quadric hypersurfaces

\displaystyle\pi^{0}\pi^{A_{1}A_{2}\ldots A_{2k+1}}=\pi^{[A_{1}}\pi^{A_{2}\ldots A_{2k+1}]},\qquad

\displaystyle k=1,\ldots,[m/2],

\displaystyle\pi^{0}\pi^{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}\ldots A_{2k}}=\pi^{[A_{1}A_{2}}\pi^{A_{3}\ldots A_{2k}]},\qquad

\displaystyle k=1,\ldots,[m/2],

(A.5)

in
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}^{2^{m}-1}
. We can therefore cover a fibre of
\mathbb{F}
with
2^{m}
open subsets
\mathcal{U}_{0}
,
\mathcal{U}_{A_{1}\ldots A_{k}}
, where
\pi^{0}\neq 0
on
\mathcal{U}_{0}
and
\pi^{A_{1}\ldots A_{k}}\neq 0
on
\mathcal{U}_{A_{1}\ldots A_{k}}
, and thus obtain
2^{m}
coordinate charts in the obvious way. This induces an atlas of charts on
\mathbb{F}_{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}}
given by the open subsets
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}\times\mathcal{U}_{0}
,
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}\times\mathcal{U}_{A_{1}\ldots A_{k}}
.

Let us now write the spinor
\omega^{\mathbf{A}}
in
\mathbb{S}_{-\frac{1}{2}}
in the Fock basis as

\displaystyle\omega^{\mathbf{A}}=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\sqrt{2}}\omega^{0}o^{\mathbf{A}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\omega^{A}\delta_{A}^{\mathbf{A}},\qquad m=1,

\displaystyle\omega^{\mathbf{A}}=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\sqrt{2}}\omega^{0}o^{\mathbf{A}}+\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2\sqrt{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{[m/2]}\left(-\frac{1}{4}\right)^{k-1}\frac{1}{(k-1)!}\omega^{A_{1}\ldots A_{2k}}\delta_{A_{1}A_{2}\ldots A_{2k}}^{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\omega^{\mathbf{A}} =}{}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{[m/2]}\left(-\frac{1}{4}\right)^{k}\frac{1}{k!}\omega^{A_{1}\ldots A_{2k+1}}\delta_{A_{1}\ldots A_{2k+1}}^{\mathbf{A}},\qquad m>1,

(A.6)

where
\omega^{0}
and
\omega^{A_{1}A_{2}\ldots A_{k}}=\omega^{[A_{1}A_{2}\ldots A_{k}]}
are the components of
\omega^{\mathbf{A}}
. The condition for
Z^{\bm{\upalpha}}=(\omega^{\mathbf{A}},\pi^{\mathbf{A}})
to be pure, so that (
2.31
) hold, is that the relations

\displaystyle\pi^{0}\omega^{A_{1}\ldots A_{2k-1}A_{2k}}=\pi^{[A_{1}\ldots A_{2k-1}}\omega^{A_{2k}]}-\tfrac{1}{2k}\pi^{A_{1}\ldots A_{2k}}\omega^{0},

\displaystyle\pi^{0}\omega^{A_{1}\ldots A_{2k}A_{2k+1}}=\pi^{[A_{1}\ldots A_{2k}}\omega^{A_{2k+1}]},

hold for
k\geq 1
when
m>1
, and that (
A.5
) hold too when
m>2
. Hence, we can cover
\mathbb{PT}_{\setminus\widehat{\infty}}
with
2^{m}
open subsets
\mathcal{V}_{0}
, where
\pi^{0}\neq 0
, and
\mathcal{V}_{A_{1}\ldots A_{k}}
where
\pi^{A_{1}\ldots A_{k}}\neq 0
in the obvious way. Coordinates on the complement
\widehat{\infty}
parametrised by
[\omega^{\mathbf{A}},0]
satisfy the conditions

\displaystyle\omega^{0}\omega^{A_{1}\ldots A_{2k}A_{2k+1}}=-2k\omega^{[A_{1}\ldots A_{2k}}\omega^{A_{2k+1}]},\qquad\omega^{[A_{1}\ldots A_{2k-1}}\omega^{A_{2k}]}=0.

Let
(z^{A},z_{A},u)
be null coordinates on
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}
in the sense that
x^{a}=z^{A}\delta^{a}_{A}+z_{A}\delta^{aA}+uu^{a}
so that the flat metric on
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}
takes the form
\bm{g}=2\mathrm{d}z^{A}\odot\mathrm{d}z_{A}+\mathrm{d}u\otimes\mathrm{d}u
. Then the incidence relation (
2.33
) reads

\displaystyle\omega^{0}=\pi^{0}u-\pi^{B}z_{B},

\displaystyle\omega^{A}=\pi^{0}z^{A}+\pi^{AB}z_{B}+\tfrac{1}{2}\pi^{A}u,

\displaystyle\omega^{A_{1}\ldots A_{2k-1}A_{2k}}=\pi^{[A_{1}\ldots A_{2k-1}}z^{A_{2k}]}+\tfrac{4k+2}{4k}\pi^{A_{1}\ldots A_{2k-1}A_{2k}A_{2k+1}}z_{A_{2k+1}}-\tfrac{1}{2k}\pi^{A_{1}\ldots A_{2k}}u,

\displaystyle\omega^{A_{1}\ldots A_{2k}A_{2k+1}}=\pi^{[A_{1}\ldots A_{2k}}z^{A_{2k+1}]}+\pi^{A_{1}\ldots A_{2k}A_{2k+1}A_{2k+2}}z_{A_{2k+2}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\pi^{A_{1}\ldots A_{2k+1}}u.

We now work in the chart
\mathcal{U}_{0}
, and since
\pi^{0}\neq 0
there, we can set with no loss of generality
\pi^{0}=1
. Let
(x,\pi)
be a point in
\mathbb{F}_{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}}
and let
(\mathcal{U}_{0},(\pi^{A},\pi^{AB}))
be a coordinate chart containing
\pi\in\mathbb{F}_{x}
. Let
(\omega,\pi)
be the image of
(x,\pi)
under the projection
\mu\colon\mathbb{F}\rightarrow\mathbb{PT}
so that
(\mathcal{V}_{0},(\omega^{0},\omega^{A},\pi^{A},\pi^{AB}))
is a coordinate chart containing
(\omega,\pi)
. Then, in these charts, (
A.6
) and (
A.4
) reduce to

\displaystyle\omega^{\mathbf{A}}=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\big(\mathrm{i}\omega^{0}o^{\mathbf{A}}+\omega^{A}\delta_{A}^{\mathbf{A}}-\tfrac{\mathrm{i}}{4}\big(\pi^{AB}\omega^{0}-2\pi^{A}\omega^{B}\big)\delta_{AB}^{\mathbf{A}}+\cdots\big),

(A.7a)

\displaystyle\pi^{\mathbf{A}}=o^{\mathbf{A}}+\tfrac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\pi^{A}\delta_{A}^{\mathbf{A}}-\tfrac{1}{4}\pi^{AB}\delta_{AB}^{\mathbf{A}}+\cdots.

(A.7b)

More succinctly,
\pi^{\mathbf{A}}=\exp(-\frac{1}{4}\pi^{ab}\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.85431pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.693pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.35213pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{A}}}}_{{{ab}{\mathbf{B}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}})o^{{{\mathbf{B}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.39859pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00844pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}
, where
\pi^{ab}=\pi^{AB}\delta^{a}_{A}\delta^{b}_{B}+2\pi^{A}\delta^{[a}_{A}u^{b]}
belongs to the complement of the stabiliser of
o^{\mathbf{A}}
in
\mathfrak{so}(\mathbb{V}_{0},g_{ab})
, i.e.,
(\pi^{A},\pi^{AB})
are coordinates on a dense open subset of the homogeneous space
P/Q
. We can also rewrite
\omega^{\mathbf{A}}
more compactly in the two alternative forms

\displaystyle\omega^{\mathbf{A}}=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\big(\omega^{A}\delta^{a}_{A}+\tfrac{1}{2}\omega^{0}u^{a}\big)\pi^{{{\mathbf{A}}}}_{{{a}}}+\tfrac{\mathrm{i}}{2\sqrt{2}}\omega^{0}\pi^{\mathbf{A}},

\displaystyle\omega^{\mathbf{A}}=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\omega^{a}\pi^{{{\mathbf{A}}}}_{{{a}}},\qquad\text{where}\quad\omega^{a}:=\big(\omega^{A}-\tfrac{1}{2}\omega^{0}\pi^{A}\big)\delta^{{{a}}}_{{{A}}}+\omega^{0}u^{a},

from which it is easy to check that
\pi^{\mathbf{A}}
and
\omega^{\mathbf{A}}
indeed satisfy the conditions given in Lemma
2.19
.

Finally, in the coordinate chart
(\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}\times\mathcal{U}_{0},(z^{A},z_{A},u;\pi^{A},\pi^{AB}))
, we have

\displaystyle x^{a}\pi_{a}^{\mathbf{A}}=\mathrm{i}\big(u-\pi^{B}z_{B}\big)o^{\mathbf{A}}+\big(z^{B}+\pi^{BC}z_{C}+\tfrac{1}{2}u\pi^{B}\big)\delta_{B}^{\mathbf{A}}+\cdots,

so that the incidence relation (
2.33
) reduces to

\displaystyle\omega^{A}=z^{A}+\pi^{AB}z_{B}+\tfrac{1}{2}\pi^{A}u,\qquad\omega^{0}=u-\pi^{B}z_{B}.

(A.8)

Tangent and cotangent spaces.
Let us introduce the short-hand notation

\displaystyle\partial_{A}:=\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{A}}=\delta^{a}_{A}\nabla_{a},\qquad\partial^{A}:=\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{A}}=\delta^{aA}\nabla_{a},\qquad\partial:=\frac{\partial}{\partial u}=u^{a}\nabla_{a},

so that
\mathrm{T}_{(x,\pi)}\mathcal{Q}^{n}\cong\mathfrak{p}_{-1}=\langle\partial_{A},\partial^{A},\partial\rangle
, and define
1
-forms

\displaystyle\bm{\alpha}^{A}:=\mathrm{d}\omega^{A}+\tfrac{1}{2}\pi^{A}\mathrm{d}\omega^{0}-\tfrac{1}{2}\omega^{0}\mathrm{d}\pi^{A},\qquad\bm{\alpha}^{AB}:=\mathrm{d}\pi^{AB}-\pi^{[A}\mathrm{d}\pi^{B]},

(A.9)

and vectors

\displaystyle\bm{X}_{A}:=\frac{\partial}{\partial\omega^{A}},\qquad

\displaystyle\bm{X}_{AB}:=\frac{\partial}{\partial\pi^{AB}},

\displaystyle\bm{Y}:=\frac{\partial}{\partial\omega^{0}}-\frac{1}{2}\pi^{C}\frac{\partial}{\partial\omega^{C}},\qquad

\displaystyle\bm{Y}_{A}:=\frac{\partial}{\partial\pi^{A}}-\pi^{B}\frac{\partial}{\partial\pi^{AB}}+\frac{1}{2}\omega^{0}\frac{\partial}{\partial\omega^{A}}.

(A.10)

Then bases for the cotangent and tangent spaces of
\mathbb{PT}
at
(\omega,\pi)
are given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{T}^{*}_{(\omega,\pi)}\mathbb{PT}\cong\mathfrak{r}^{*}_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{r}^{*}_{2}=\big\langle\mathrm{d}\omega^{0},\mathrm{d}\pi^{A}\big\rangle\oplus\big\langle\bm{\alpha}^{A},\bm{\alpha}^{AB}\big\rangle,

\displaystyle\mathrm{T}_{(\omega,\pi)}\mathbb{PT}\cong\mathfrak{r}_{-2}\oplus\mathfrak{r}_{-1}=\big\langle\bm{X}_{A}\bm{X}_{AB}\big\rangle\oplus\big\langle\bm{Y},\bm{Y}_{A}\big\rangle,

respectively.

Remark A.1
.

Using (
A.7
), one can check that the expressions for the set (
A.9
) of
\frac{1}{2}m(m+1)

1
-forms are none other than the
1
-forms (
2.32
), and thus (
2.25
). These forms annihilate the rank-
(m+1)
canonical distribution
\mathrm{D}
on
\mathbb{PT}
spanned by
\bm{Y}
and
\bm{Y}_{A}
. Further, the vector
\bm{Y}
clearly coincides with (
2.37
) to describe mini-twistor space – this can be checked by using transformations (
A.7
).

Now, define the
1
-forms and vectors

\displaystyle\bm{\theta}^{A}:=\mathrm{d}z^{A}+\big(\pi^{AD}-\tfrac{1}{2}\pi^{A}\pi^{D}\big)\mathrm{d}z_{D}+\pi^{A}\mathrm{d}u,\qquad

\displaystyle\bm{\theta}^{0}:=\mathrm{d}u-\pi^{C}\mathrm{d}z_{C},

\displaystyle\bm{Z}^{A}:=\partial^{A}+\big(\pi^{AD}-\tfrac{1}{2}\pi^{A}\pi^{D}\big)\partial_{D}+\pi^{A}\partial,\qquad

\displaystyle\bm{U}:=\partial-\pi^{D}\partial_{D},

\displaystyle\bm{W}_{A}:=\frac{\partial}{\partial\pi^{A}}-\pi^{B}\frac{\partial}{\partial\pi^{AB}}.

Then bases for the cotangent and tangent spaces of
\mathbb{F}
at
(x,\pi)
are given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{T}^{*}_{(x,\pi)}\mathbb{F}\cong{\mathfrak{q}^{*}_{1}}^{E}\oplus{\mathfrak{q}_{1}^{*}}^{F}\oplus{\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{*}}^{E}\oplus{\mathfrak{q}_{2}^{*}}^{F}\oplus\mathfrak{q}_{3}^{*}=\langle\mathrm{d}z_{A}\rangle\oplus\big\langle\mathrm{d}\pi^{A}\big\rangle\oplus\langle\bm{\theta}^{0}\rangle\oplus\big\langle\bm{\alpha}^{AB}\big\rangle\oplus\big\langle\bm{\theta}^{A}\big\rangle,

\displaystyle\mathrm{T}_{(x,\pi)}\mathbb{F}\cong\mathfrak{q}_{-3}\oplus\mathfrak{q}_{-2}^{F}\oplus\mathfrak{q}_{-2}^{E}\oplus\mathfrak{q}_{-1}^{F}\oplus\mathfrak{q}_{-1}^{E}=\langle\partial_{A}\rangle\oplus\langle\bm{X}_{AB}\rangle\oplus\langle\bm{U}\rangle\oplus\langle\bm{W}_{A}\rangle\oplus\big\langle\bm{Z}^{A}\big\rangle,

respectively.

We note that the coordinates
(\omega^{0},\omega^{A},\pi^{A},\pi^{AB})
on
\mathcal{V}_{0}
are indeed annihilated by the vectors
\bm{Z}^{A}
tangent to the fibres of
\mathbb{F}\rightarrow\mathbb{PT}
. Further, the pullback of
\bm{\alpha}^{A}
to
\mathbb{F}
is given by
\mu^{*}(\bm{\alpha}^{A})=\bm{\alpha}^{AB}z_{B}+\bm{\theta}^{A}
, i.e., the annihilator of
{\mathrm{D}}=\mathrm{T}^{-1}\mathbb{PT}
pulls back to the annihilator of
\mathrm{T}^{-2}_{E}\mathbb{F}
corresponding to
\mathfrak{q}_{-2}^{E}\oplus\mathfrak{q}_{-1}^{F}\oplus\mathfrak{q}_{-1}^{E}
.

Mini-twistor space.
By Lemma
2.24
, the mini-twistor space
\mathbb{M}\mathbb{T}
of
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}
is the leaf space of the vector field
\bm{Y}
defined by (
2.37
), given in (
A.10
) in the coordinate chart
(\mathcal{V}_{0},(\omega^{0},\omega^{A},\pi^{A}
,
\pi^{AB}))
. Accordingly, we have a local coordinate chart
(\underline{\mathcal{V}}_{0},(\underline{\omega}^{A},\pi^{AB},\pi^{A}))
on
\mathbb{M}\mathbb{T}
where

\displaystyle\underline{\omega}^{A}=\omega^{A}+\tfrac{1}{2}\pi^{A}\omega^{0},

which can be seen to be annihilated by
\bm{Y}
. The incidence relation (
2.35
) or (
2.40
) can then be expressed as

\displaystyle\underline{\omega}^{A}=z^{A}+\big(\pi^{AB}-\tfrac{1}{2}\pi^{A}\pi^{B}\big)z_{B}+\pi^{A}u,

which are indeed annihilated by
\bm{Z}^{A}
and
\bm{U}
. The tangent space of
\mathbb{M}\mathbb{T}
at a point
(\underline{\omega},\pi)
in
\underline{\mathcal{V}}_{0}
is clearly

\displaystyle\mathrm{T}_{(\underline{\omega},\pi)}\mathbb{M}\mathbb{T}=\langle\underline{\bm{X}}_{A},\bm{X}_{AB},\bm{W}_{A}\rangle,\qquad\text{where}\quad\underline{\bm{X}}_{A}:=\frac{\partial}{\partial\underline{\omega}^{A}}.

Normal bundle of
\hat{x}
in
\mathbb{PT}_{\setminus\widehat{\infty}}
.
Let
x
be a point in
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{E}^{n}
. In the chart
(\mathcal{V}_{0},(\omega^{0},\omega^{A},\pi^{A},\pi^{AB}))
, the corresponding
\hat{x}
is given by (
A.8
). In particular, the
1
-forms

\displaystyle\bm{\beta}^{A}(x):=\mathrm{d}\omega^{A}-\mathrm{d}\pi^{AB}z_{B}-\tfrac{1}{2}\mathrm{d}\pi^{A}u,\qquad\bm{\beta}^{0}(x):=\mathrm{d}\omega^{0}+\mathrm{d}\pi^{B}z_{B},

vanish on
\hat{x}
, and the tangent space of
\hat{x}
at
(\omega,\pi)
is spanned by the vectors
\bm{Y}_{A}-z_{A}\bm{Y}
and
\bm{X}_{AB}-z_{[A}\bm{X}_{B]}
. This distinguishes the
m
-dimensional subspace
\langle\bm{Y}_{A}-z_{A}\bm{Y}\rangle
tangent to both
\hat{x}
and the canonical distribution
{\mathrm{D}}
at
(\omega,\pi)
.

A.2
Even dimensions

The local description of
\mathbb{F}
and
\mathbb{PT}
in even dimensions can be easily derived from the one above.
We split
\mathbb{V}_{0}
as
\mathbb{V}_{0}\cong\mathbb{W}\oplus\mathbb{W}^{*}
where
\mathbb{W}\cong\mathbb{C}^{m}
is a totally null
m
-plane of
(\mathbb{V}_{0},g_{ab})
, with adapted basis
\{\delta^{aA},\delta^{a}_{A}\}
. The Fock representations of the irreducible spinor spaces
\mathbb{S}_{-\frac{1}{2}}
and
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}_{-\frac{1}{2}}
on
\mathbb{V}_{0}
are given by

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}_{\frac{1}{2}}\cong\mathbb{S}^{\prime}_{-\frac{1}{2}}\cong\wedge^{m}\mathbb{W}\oplus\wedge^{m-2}\mathbb{W}\oplus\cdots,\qquad\mathbb{S}^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{2}}\cong\mathbb{S}_{-\frac{1}{2}}\cong\wedge^{m-1}\mathbb{W}\oplus\wedge^{m-3}\mathbb{W}\oplus\cdots.

Let
o^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}
be a (pure) spinor annihilating
\mathbb{W}
. Then bases for
\mathbb{S}_{\frac{1}{2}}
and
\mathbb{S}_{-\frac{1}{2}}
can then be produced by acting on
o^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}
by basis elements of
\wedge^{2k}\mathbb{W}^{*}
and of
\wedge^{2k-1}\mathbb{W}^{*}
. Explicitly,

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}_{\frac{1}{2}}=\big\langle o^{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}},\delta^{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}}_{A_{1}A_{2}},\ldots\big\rangle,\qquad\mathbb{S}_{-\frac{1}{2}}=\big\langle\delta^{\mathbf{A}}_{A_{1}},\delta^{\mathbf{A}}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}},\ldots\big\rangle,

where

\displaystyle\delta^{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}}_{A_{1}\ldots A_{2k}}:=\delta_{[A_{1}}^{a_{1}}\cdots\delta_{A_{2k}]}^{a_{2k}}o^{\mathbf{A_{0}^{\prime}}}\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.09247pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.09247pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.77274pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55551pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{A_{1}}}}}_{{{a_{1}}{\mathbf{A_{0}^{\prime}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.09247pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.09247pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.77274pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55551pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\cdots\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.99284pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.99284pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.77237pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.03233pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[17.51727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[17.51727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[12.07616pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.85893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}}}}_{{{a_{2k}}{\mathbf{A_{2k-1}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.62248pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.62248pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72275pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.50552pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}},

\displaystyle\delta^{\mathbf{A}}_{A_{1}\ldots A_{2k-1}}:=\delta_{[A_{1}}^{a_{1}}\cdots\delta_{A_{2k-1}]}^{a_{2k-1}}o^{\mathbf{A_{0}^{\prime}}}\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.28764pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.84009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.09247pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.09247pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.77274pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55551pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{A_{1}}}}}_{{{a_{1}}{\mathbf{A_{0}^{\prime}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.09247pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.09247pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.77274pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55551pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\cdots\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.07617pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.07617pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.94899pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[17.51727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[17.51727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[12.07616pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.85893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mathbf{A}}}}_{{{a_{2k-1}}{\mathbf{A_{2k-2}^{\prime}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.775pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26025pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04303pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}.

The Clifford action of
\mathbb{V}_{0}\subset{\mathcal{C}\ell}(\mathbb{V}_{0},g_{ab})
on
\mathbb{S}_{\pm\frac{1}{2}}
follows the same lines as (
A.3
) with appropriate priming of spinor indices.

Coordinate charts in even dimensions can be obtained from the odd-dimensional case by switching off
\pi^{A_{1}\ldots A_{k}}
for all odd
k
, and
\omega^{A_{1}\ldots A_{k}}
for all even
k
. We therefore have a covering of each fibre of
\mathbb{F}
by
2^{m-1}
open subsets
\mathcal{U}_{0}
,
\mathcal{U}_{A_{1}\ldots A_{2k}}
, and a covering of
\mathbb{PT}_{\setminus\widehat{\infty}}
by
2^{m-1}
open subsets
\mathcal{V}_{0}
,
\mathcal{V}_{A_{1}\ldots A_{2k}}
in the obvious way. In particular, in
(\mathcal{V}_{0},(\omega^{A},\pi^{AB}))
, the homogeneous coordinates
[\omega^{\mathbf{A}},\pi^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}]
are given by

\displaystyle\omega^{\mathbf{A}}=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\big(\omega^{A}\delta_{A}^{\mathbf{A}}-\tfrac{1}{4}\omega^{A}\pi^{BC}\delta_{ABC}^{\mathbf{A}}+\cdots\big),\qquad\pi^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}=o^{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}}-\tfrac{1}{4}\pi^{AB}\delta_{AB}^{\mathbf{A^{\prime}}}+\cdots.

where the former can also be rewritten as
\omega^{\mathbf{A}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\omega^{a}\pi_{a}^{\mathbf{A}}
with
\omega^{a}:=\omega^{A}\delta_{A}^{a}
. Finally, the even-dimensional version of the incidence relation (
2.33
) can be rewritten as
\omega^{A}=z^{A}+\pi^{AB}z_{B}
.

As for the tangent spaces of
\mathcal{Q}^{2m}
, its twistor space and their correspondence space, we find, in the obvious notation,
\mathrm{T}_{(x,\pi)}\mathcal{Q}^{n}\cong\mathfrak{p}_{-1}=\langle\partial_{A},\partial^{A},\partial\rangle
,
\mathrm{T}_{(x,\pi)}\mathbb{F}\cong\mathfrak{q}_{-2}\oplus\mathfrak{q}_{-1}^{F}\oplus\mathfrak{q}_{-1}^{E}=\langle\partial_{A}\rangle\oplus\langle\bm{X}_{AB}\rangle\oplus\langle\bm{Z}^{A}\rangle
, and
\mathrm{T}_{(\omega,\pi)}\mathbb{PT}\cong\mathfrak{r}_{-1}=\langle\bm{X}_{A},\bm{X}_{AB}\rangle
, where
\bm{Z}^{A}:=\partial^{A}+\pi^{AB}\partial_{B}
,
\bm{X}_{AB}:=\frac{\partial}{\partial\pi^{AB}}
,
\bm{X}_{A}:=\frac{\partial}{\partial\omega^{A}}
, and so on.
